{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JVSCreVRCwBxJi7GGQmop1","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# cliEval — Architectural Decisions Log\n2\t\n3\t**Status:** 🟡 PROPOSED-R2 2026-05-18 (pending maintainer sign-off)\n4\t**Format:** ADR-lite (one decision per section: Context → Options → Decision → Consequences)\n5\t\n6\t**Revision log:**\n7\t- R1 (2026-05-18): D-1..D-4 initial proposals.\n8\t- R2 (2026-05-18): D-5..D-8 added to resolve the 4 CRITICAL findings from `spec-panel-review.md`.\n9\t- R3 (2026-05-20): OPS-001 closure — D-5..D-8 status flipped to \"queued for sign-off\"; OQ-1/3/7/8/10 resolution-status block added; implementation gates cross-referenced to ADR IDs. See §OPS-001 Closure below; per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0021/spec.md`.\n10\t- R4 (2026-05-20): DOC-OQ4 closure — D-10 added recording the NOTICE/LICENSE attribution mechanism for the vendored ptytest fork. OQ-4 status flips from OPEN to RESOLVED (M2 entry blocker for T02.01 cleared); per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0024/spec.md`.\n11\t\n12\t---\n13\t\n14\t## D-1: PTY layer — fork ptytest vs build minimal pexpect wrapper\n15\t\n16\t### Context\n17\t\n18\tReal Claude Code is a TTY-based interactive REPL. The eval harness needs a PTY (pseudo-terminal) driver that can spawn it, write to its stdin, read from its stdout/stderr, detect \"prompt ready\" states, kill on timeout, and capture exit codes.\n19\t\n20\t### Options\n21\t\n22\t| Option | Pros | Cons | LOC | Risk |\n23\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n24\t| **A. Fork `brandon-fryslie/ptytest`** | Working Claude Code example already exists in repo. Plugin-free standalone class. Active enough (recent commits). | 1 star / 0 forks → bus-factor real. Includes pytest-fixture machinery we don't need. | ~300 LOC adaptation | LOW (ptytest is small, easy to own) |\n25\t| B. Build minimal `pexpect` wrapper from scratch | Clean ownership, no upstream-drift risk, exactly the API we want. | More code to write, debug, test. We re-derive Claude Code TTY quirks. | ~700 LOC fresh | MEDIUM (pexpect has subtle gotchas) |\n26\t| C. Use `pexpect` directly (no wrapper) | Zero abstraction overhead. | Every eval re-implements spawn/wait/expect_prompt patterns; code duplication. | ~0 LOC harness; +~50 LOC per eval | HIGH (drift across evals) |\n27\t\n28\t### Decision: **A — fork ptytest, vendor under `cli/eval/pty/`, fork-and-own posture.**\n29\t\n30\t### Rationale\n31\t\n32\t1. The fork-research report verified ptytest has a working Claude Code example, which means the hardest TTY edge cases (prompt detection, ANSI escape handling, line-buffered reads) are already solved.\n33\t2. Bus-factor risk is mitigated by **vendoring** (we copy the code in; no runtime pip dependency on the upstream repo). If upstream dies, we have a frozen working copy.\n34\t3. The durable dependency is `pexpect` (2.8k stars, active again at v4.9) — ptytest is just a thin layer on top.\n35\t4. 300 LOC adaptation is ~5x cheaper than 700 LOC fresh build.\n36\t5. Option C (raw pexpect) fails the harness-cleanliness goal — evals should look like assertion code, not PTY plumbing.\n37\t\n38\t### Consequences\n39\t\n40\t- We own a small vendored fork forever. `PROVENANCE.md` documents the fork SHA and our diffs.\n41\t- Quarterly review of upstream `pexpect` releases; resync if security-relevant.\n42\t- A new file at `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` reproducing upstream MIT terms (license compliance).\n43\t\n44\t---\n45\t\n46\t## D-2: Assertion DSL — adopt `Expect.*` port vs raw assertions\n47\t\n48\t### Context\n49\t\n50\tEach eval needs to verify hook side-effects (file presence, JSONL events, settings.json registrations, exit codes, TTY output). The expression of these assertions can be:\n51\t\n52\t### Options\n53\t\n54\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n55\t|---|---|---|\n56\t| **A. Port `mcp-eval`'s `Expect.tools.*` DSL idea as Python primitives** | Declarative YAML manifest possible. Reusable across evals. Single place to add new assertion types. Mental-model match with industry pattern. | +~200 LOC for the DSL itself. Some abstraction overhead (declarative → callable). |\n57\t| B. Raw Python `assert` statements in each eval | Lowest abstraction. No new code. | Each eval is more code. Declarative YAML manifest not possible (or only partial). Failure messages are ad-hoc per eval. |\n58\t| C. Use pytest's assertion machinery directly | Free; idiomatic. | Couples harness to pytest, but the harness is NOT pytest-driven (it's a CLI subcommand). |\n59\t\n60\t### Decision: **A — port `Expect.*` DSL, ~200 LOC, no upstream dependency.**\n61\t\n62\t### Rationale\n63\t\n64\t1. The YAML manifest schema (§5 of design-spec) is a hard requirement to keep eval definitions declarative and reviewable. Raw Python assertions can't be expressed in YAML.\n65\t2. A small DSL (~10 method types: `file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) covers all 15 evals comfortably.\n66\t3. The mcp-eval API surface is a proven mental model — we steal the ergonomics without taking a dependency on the framework (which has the wrong architecture for our needs).\n67\t4. Failure messages from a typed DSL are uniformly high-quality (the DSL knows its own structure); ad-hoc assertions tend to produce noisy error output.\n68\t5. The DSL has a programmatic escape hatch for parameterized evals that can't be expressed in YAML — so we don't lock ourselves out of complex assertions.\n69\t\n70\t### Consequences\n71\t\n72\t- `cli/eval/expect.py` is a public API surface; changes need backward-compat care once evals are written against it.\n73\t- The YAML schema (`suites/suite.schema.json`) and the DSL must stay in lockstep — `loader.py` translates declarative → callable.\n74\t- Out-of-scope for v1: LLM-judge assertions (mcp-eval has these; we don't need them for hook validation).\n75\t\n76\t---\n77\t\n78\t## D-3: HOME isolation — extend existing `IsolationLayers` vs separate class\n79\t\n80\t### Context\n81\t\n82\t`src/superclaude/cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182` already implements 4-layer per-subprocess isolation (`CLAUDE_WORK_DIR`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DIR`, `CLAUDE_SETTINGS_DIR`). The eval harness needs a 5th layer: `HOME` override (and `XDG_*` companions). Question: extend the existing class or build a new one?\n83\t\n84\t### Options\n85\t\n86\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n87\t|---|---|---|\n88\t| **A. Compose `IsolationLayers` in a new `HomeIsolation` class** | Reuses 4 existing layers for free. No risk to sprint's existing usage. New `HOME` layer lives in eval code, not sprint code. | Two classes to maintain. Slight indirection. |\n89\t| B. Add `HOME` layer to `IsolationLayers` directly (modify sprint code) | One unified class. Sprint also benefits from HOME isolation (theoretical future use). | Modifies code outside this release's scope. Sprint doesn't currently need HOME isolation, so YAGNI risk. Sprint regression risk. |\n90\t| C. Build `HomeIsolation` standalone (no inheritance/composition) | Maximum decoupling. | Duplicate 4 layers of code. Bug-fixes diverge over time. |\n91\t\n92\t### Decision: **A — `HomeIsolation` COMPOSES `IsolationLayers` (has-a, not is-a).**\n93\t\n94\t### Rationale\n95\t\n96\t1. Composition preserves sprint's existing class verbatim. Zero risk to PR #49-era infrastructure.\n97\t2. The `HOME` layer is conceptually a \"5th layer,\" but its lifecycle (mkdtemp → install hooks → seed state → teardown) is more complex than the existing 4 (which are just env-var settings). Keeping it separate cleanly captures that asymmetry.\n98\t3. If a future sprint feature ever needs HOME isolation, it can either re-use `HomeIsolation` directly or migrate the logic upward — easier path than splitting later.\n99\t4. The interface in `design-spec.md` §7 already reflects this: `HomeIsolation` exposes `.env()` that returns the dict overlay, which the caller merges with `IsolationLayers.env()` output.\n100\t\n101\t### Consequences\n102\t\n103\t- `HomeIsolation` constructor takes an `IsolationLayers` instance as a dependency.\n104\t- Documentation in `cli/eval/isolation.py` cross-references `cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182`.\n105\t- If the sprint `IsolationLayers` API ever changes shape, we have a single integration point to update.\n106\t\n107\t---\n108\t\n109\t## D-4: Eval registry — YAML manifest vs Python-decorator-registered\n110\t\n111\t### Context\n112\t\n113\tThe 15 evals need to be defined somewhere. The orchestrator needs to enumerate them, validate dependencies, schedule them, and report results. Options diverge on where the \"source of truth\" lives.\n114\t\n115\t### Options\n116\t\n117\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n118\t|---|---|---|\n119\t| **A. YAML manifests under `suites/*.yaml`** | Declarative, human-readable, reviewable diffs, schema-validated, easy to add new suites (`fast.yaml`, `nightly.yaml`). Non-Python contributors can author/review evals. | Some Python required for complex pre/post logic; needs an \"escape hatch\" mechanism. |\n120\t| B. Python-decorator-registered (`@eval(id=\"E1\", title=\"...\", requires=[...])`) | Maximum flexibility — eval bodies are first-class Python. Type-checking friendly. | Diff churn higher (Python > YAML in PR review noise). Adding new suites requires more boilerplate. Schema enforcement is via type hints, less ergonomic for non-Python contributors. |\n121\t| C. Hybrid — YAML defines metadata, Python `@assertion_callback` for escape hatch | Best of both worlds for simple+complex evals. | More mechanism to learn; two source-of-truth surfaces. |\n122\t\n123\t### Decision: **A — YAML manifest as primary, with optional Python escape-hatch via `callback:` field for parameterized/complex evals.**\n124\t\n125\t### Rationale\n126\t\n127\t1. **Reviewability** is paramount: a PR adding a new eval should be readable as a YAML diff, not as a Python class with overrides. The 15-eval suite is meant to be a living spec — declarative beats imperative.\n128\t2. The schema (§5 of design-spec) covers the common case (file/JSONL/settings/exit/stderr/duration assertions) elegantly.\n129\t3. The `callback:` field provides escape-hatch power without forcing every eval through it:\n130\t   ```yaml\n131\t   - id: E14\n132\t     title: \"concurrent SessionStart bursts\"\n133\t     callback: superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start\n134\t   ```\n135\t   The callback is invoked only when an eval's logic can't be expressed in YAML (E14, possibly E15).\n136\t4. New suites (`fast.yaml`, `regression.yaml`, etc.) drop in as data files with no code changes — exactly the kind of low-friction extension we want.\n137\t5. `loader.py` validates against `suites/suite.schema.json` once; the rest of the pipeline trusts validated input.\n138\t\n139\t### Consequences\n140\t\n141\t- `suites/suite.schema.json` is a public artifact (committed) — schema changes need versioning.\n142\t- Callbacks live in `suites/<suite-name>_callbacks.py` co-located with the YAML.\n143\t- Eval IDs (`E1`, `E2.1`, etc.) are stable identifiers — must not be renamed without a migration note.\n144\t- Parameterized evals (e.g., E2's 3 prefix variants) use a `parameterize:` block in YAML; the loader expands these into separate `EvalSpec` instances at load time.\n145\t\n146\t---\n147\t\n148\t## D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate — falsifiable definition of \"equivalent regression\"\n149\t\n150\t### Context\n151\t\n152\tGoal G5 in design-spec §1 said \"catch the bug PR #49 fixed and any equivalent future regression\" — but \"equivalent\" was undefined. Spec-panel finding W-1 (CRITICAL) flagged this as unfalsifiable: with no measurable acceptance criterion, the harness has no way to answer \"did I do my job?\"\n153\t\n154\t### Options\n155\t\n156\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n157\t|---|---|---|\n158\t| **A. Coverage gate: every matcher P in `hooks.json` must have ≥1 eval in the loaded suite** | Falsifiable, mechanically enforceable. CI-friendly. Maps to the actual class of bugs PR #49 lived in (matcher↔case-body drift). | Requires `eval doctor --check-coverage`. New eval must accompany every new hook matcher. |\n159\t| B. Snapshot match — assert this commit's matchers equal a frozen golden list | Simple. | Frozen list goes stale; bug-class definition is \"matches a list\" not \"real-world hook fires.\" |\n160\t| C. Leave G5 prose-only (\"intent is clear enough\") | Zero work. | Spec-panel CRITICAL stands; downstream tasklist has no test for G5. |\n161\t\n162\t### Decision: **A — coverage gate enforced by loader + `eval doctor --check-coverage`.**\n163\t\n164\t### Rationale\n165\t\n166\t1. The class of bug PR #49 introduced was \"matcher exists in `hooks.json` but no real-world test exercises it.\" A coverage gate directly inverts that condition.\n167\t2. Mechanical enforcement at load time means the harness fails fast (exit 2) when a new matcher lands without an eval — no silent drift.\n168\t3. The gate runs in two places: (a) `eval doctor` for pre-flight, (b) top of `eval run` for the live run. Both are cheap (single pass over `hooks.json` + manifest).\n169\t\n170\t### Consequences\n171\t\n172\t- `loader.py` reads `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` at run-start, extracts every PostToolUse matcher pattern, and verifies ≥1 eval in the manifest declares `inputs.expect_tool_call` matching each pattern.\n173\t- New hook matcher PRs must include a paired eval — enforced by a CI lint (deferred per non-goals but the harness itself enforces locally).\n174\t- Acceptable escape hatch: manifest declares `coverage_exempt_matchers: [\"<pattern>\"]` with a `reason:` field for matchers intentionally untestable.\n175\t\n176\t---\n177\t\n178\t## D-6: Disk-budget enforcement strategy\n179\t\n180\t### Context\n181\t\n182\tSpec-panel finding N-2 (CRITICAL) flagged internal inconsistency: §14 R4 cited `--max-disk-mb` as the mitigation but §4 flag table omitted the flag. Either add it or strike the mitigation.\n183\t\n184\t### Options\n185\t\n186\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n187\t|---|---|---|\n188\t| **A. Add `--max-disk-mb` flag (default 1024), poll every 5s, abort-on-breach** | Closes the cited risk. Bounded blast radius. Simple implementation. | One more flag. Polling adds tiny CPU overhead. |\n189\t| B. Strike the mitigation from R4; document \"user must monitor disk manually\" | Zero code. | R4 becomes a known accepted risk; eval runs on a constrained box can fill disk. |\n190\t| C. Hard limit via `setrlimit(RLIMIT_FSIZE)` on each subprocess | Kernel enforced. | Per-process limit, not cumulative; doesn't catch the 15-parallel-evals case. |\n191\t\n192\t### Decision: **A — add `--max-disk-mb` flag with 5-second polling.**\n193\t\n194\t### Rationale\n195\t\n196\t1. The risk is real: 15 parallel evals × keep-home-on-failure × TTY transcripts can fill a small disk silently. A polling guard with graceful abort (in-flight evals complete, new ones blocked) preserves results-so-far.\n197\t2. 5-second polling is cheap: one `du -sb` (or Python `os.walk` accumulator) per tick. At 15-parallel, the orchestrator is mostly blocked on pexpect anyway.\n198\t3. Default of 1024 MB matches design-spec §12 sizing (~300 MB total artifacts at 15-eval max-keep). Generous but not unbounded.\n199\t\n200\t### Consequences\n201\t\n202\t- New flag `--max-disk-mb INT` in §4 flag table; default 1024; `0` disables.\n203\t- New orchestrator component `DiskBudgetWatchdog` running in a sidecar thread.\n204\t- New exit-2 reason code: `disk_budget_exceeded`.\n205\t- Summary.json gains `harness.peak_disk_mb` field (also satisfies spec-panel H-1 partial).\n206\t\n207\t---\n208\t\n209\t## D-7: Path-traversal hardening for HomeIsolation\n210\t\n211\t### Context\n212\t\n213\tSpec-panel finding Wh-1 (CRITICAL) demonstrated that a malformed `eval_id` (e.g., `../../../etc/passwd` or `{session_id}` self-reference) could cause `HomeIsolation` to write outside the scratch root. The §14 R7 \"Hard guard\" was prose-only and ambiguous about whether `..` traversal was resolved before the containment check.\n214\t\n215\t### Options\n216\t\n217\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n218\t|---|---|---|\n219\t| **A. Defense-in-depth: loader regex + HomeIsolation `Path.resolve().is_relative_to()` + symlink-resolution after creation + scratch_root allowlist** | Catches the attack at three independent layers. Fails closed at every layer. | More code (~30 LOC). Three checks to maintain. |\n220\t| B. Loader-only regex validation | Simple. | Single layer = single failure point. |\n221\t| C. Trust the manifest author (no validation) | Zero work. | Manifest is data; data ingestion without validation is the original sin of basically every security incident. |\n222\t\n223\t### Decision: **A — three-layer defense-in-depth (regex + Path containment + symlink resolution + allowlist).**\n224\t\n225\t### Rationale\n226\t\n227\t1. The harness operates on user-supplied YAML and writes to disk. This is the textbook injection surface. Single-layer validation is industry-known-insufficient.\n228\t2. Each layer catches a distinct attack class: regex blocks `..` and template tokens; `Path.resolve()` normalizes any survivor; `is_relative_to(scratch_root)` enforces containment; symlink resolution catches the \"scratch dir is a symlink to `$HOME`\" race; allowlist on `scratch_root` itself blocks `--output-dir /home/user/.claude`.\n229\t3. Python 3.10+ (project minimum) provides `Path.is_relative_to()` natively.\n230\t4. The performance cost is negligible (4 stat calls per eval setup).\n231\t\n232\t### Consequences\n233\t\n234\t- `HomeIsolation.setup()` gains a documented pre-flight sequence (regex re-check → resolve → contain → symlink-resolve → write).\n235\t- `EvalConfig` gains an `allowed_scratch_roots: tuple[Path, ...]` field, defaulting to `(/tmp/eval-runs, <repo>/.dev/eval-runs)`.\n236\t- New exception type `HomeContainmentViolation` (subclass of `EvalHarnessError`); maps to status ERRORED.\n237\t- Test `test_isolation.py` MUST include: (a) `..` traversal, (b) absolute-path eval_id, (c) symlink-to-home attack, (d) `--output-dir` outside allowlist.\n238\t\n239\t---\n240\t\n241\t## D-8: Reporter dimensional invariant (consumes N', not K)\n242\t\n243\t### Context\n244\t\n245\tSpec-panel Pipeline-CRITICAL showed §9's summary.json example reported `14 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped` (totals = 15, the full suite) but the spec text described capability filtering as removing evals from the schedule. If the reporter only sees the K-kept subset, a `--no-mcp` run that drops 5 evals would show \"10 passed\" with no audit trail of the 5 skipped — a silent regression in coverage measurement.\n246\t\n247\t### Options\n248\t\n249\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n250\t|---|---|---|\n251\t| **A. Orchestrator emits N' EvalOutcomes (one per expanded EvalSpec); SKIPPED carries reason; reporter asserts `len(outcomes) == N'`** | Audit trail preserved. Coverage queries answerable. Contract is checkable. | Slightly more state per skipped eval. |\n252\t| B. Reporter takes K outcomes + a separate skipped-list; merges at render time | Same audit trail. | Two channels to keep in sync. More places to drop a skip. |\n253\t| C. Status quo (K only, skip count is metadata) | Less code. | The spec-panel CRITICAL stands; this is the bug being filed. |\n254\t\n255\t### Decision: **A — single list of N' EvalOutcome records; reporter asserts dimensional invariant.**\n256\t\n257\t### Rationale\n258\t\n259\t1. A single dataclass list eliminates the \"two channels in sync\" failure mode of option B.\n260\t2. The contract assertion (`len(outcomes) == counts.expanded_n_prime`) makes the invariant grep-able and test-able; CI can verify it doesn't regress.\n261\t3. Status taxonomy expansion (8 statuses with explicit FAIL/ERRORED/TIMEOUT/INTERRUPTED/SKIPPED/XFAIL/XPASS distinction) is a natural consequence — addresses the spec-panel \"failure-mode taxonomy is sparse\" consensus point.\n262\t4. Performance cost is zero in practice (the SKIPPED records are empty-shell; N' is at most 17 with parameterize expansion).\n263\t\n264\t### Consequences\n265\t\n266\t- New dataclass `EvalOutcome` in `models.py` (see design-spec §9 \"Aggregator data contract\").\n267\t- New status values `ERRORED`, `INTERRUPTED`, `XFAIL`, `XPASS` (in addition to PASS/FAIL/TIMEOUT/SKIPPED) — taxonomy frozen in design-spec §9.\n268\t- New error `ReporterContractViolation` raised on dimensional mismatch at `AggregatedRunReport.from_outcomes()`.\n269\t- Summary.json gains `counts` block (manifest_n, expanded_n_prime, kept_k, skipped_s).\n270\t- Exit-code mapping in §4 cross-references this taxonomy.\n271\t\n272\t---\n273\t\n274\t## Cross-cutting decision: implementation order\n275\t\n276\t(Not a strict ADR, but worth recording.) Per §17 of `design-spec.md`, the implementation phases are:\n277\t\n278\t1. **Phase 1** (1 day): vendored `pty/`, `HomeIsolation`, capability gates, `eval doctor`. Smoke: `superclaude eval doctor` green.\n279\t2. **Phase 2** (1 day): `loader.py`, `models.py`, `expect.py`, `eval describe/list`. Smoke: parse a 1-eval manifest.\n280\t3. **Phase 3** (1 day): `orchestrator.py`, `runner.py`, `reporter.py`, `eval run`. Smoke: 1 real Claude Code session driven end-to-end.\n281\t4. **Phase 4** (0.5 day): wire into `cli/main.py`, Makefile target, .gitignore. Smoke: `make verify-sync` still EXIT=0.\n282\t5. **Phase 5** (1-2 weeks): implement E1-E15 in 3-5 PR batches.\n283\t\n284\t**Rationale for this ordering:** Phase 1 is the riskiest (PTY mechanics). Validating the harness can spawn-and-observe ONE Claude Code session is the gate that determines whether the entire approach is viable. If Phase 1 fails (e.g., Claude Code's TTY behavior breaks pexpect-style detection), we re-pivot before committing to the rest of the architecture.\n285\t\n286\t---\n287\t\n288\t## Sign-off\n289\t\n290\t| Decision | Status | Signed | Date |\n291\t|---|---|---|---|\n292\t| D-1: PTY layer = fork ptytest | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n293\t| D-2: Assertion DSL = Expect.* port | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n294\t| D-3: HOME isolation = compose IsolationLayers | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n295\t| D-4: Eval registry = YAML + callback escape | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n296\t| D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate (G5 falsifiable) | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n297\t| D-6: `--max-disk-mb` poller (R4 enforcement) | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n298\t| D-7: Three-layer path-traversal hardening | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n299\t| D-8: Reporter consumes N' + status taxonomy | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n300\t| D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution for vendored ptytest | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R4) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n301\t\n302\t**Maintainer:** mark each row 🟢 APPROVED + sign + date before invoking `/sc:roadmap`.\n303\t\n304\t**Reject/revise:** add a row to a \"Revisions\" section below; do not edit the original decisions in-place.\n305\t\n306\t---\n307\t\n308\t## Revisions\n309\t\n310\t(none yet)\n311\t\n312\t---\n313\t\n314\t## D-9: Reconcile `validation.status: \"fail\"` label and document manual-triage policy for unclassified deviations\n315\t\n316\t### Context\n317\t\n318\t`.roadmap-state.json:113-117` reports `fidelity_status: \"pass\"` and `validation.status: \"fail\"` (timestamp 2026-05-18T19:47:24Z, six minutes after the final pipeline step completed). However, every pipeline step in `steps.*` — including `spec-fidelity`, `wiring-verification`, `deviation-analysis`, and `remediate` — recorded `status: \"PASS\"`. `spec-fidelity.md:1-7` likewise records 0 HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW deviations and PASS convergence.\n319\t\n320\tThe `validation.status: \"fail\"` label was stamped by a separate validator step that reacts to the 20 records in `spec-deviations.md`. Per `spec-deviations.md:10`, \"deviation classification is not yet implemented. All records currently render as UNCLASSIFIED.\" `remediation-tasklist.md:14-34` then escalates all 20 to `BLOCKING` with owner `UNKNOWN` purely because the classifier never ran — not because spec-fidelity actually regressed.\n321\t\n322\tA manual cross-check (performed during this reconciliation) confirms:\n323\t\n324\t- All 15 spec→roadmap \"orphan\" findings (4 files + 11 `Expect.*` predicate helpers) ARE present in `roadmap.md` — the files appear at `roadmap.md:102,142,409-412` and the 11 helpers are enumerated in row `COMP-010` at `roadmap.md:77`. The analyzer's bare-identifier string match missed qualified references (e.g., `Expect.jsonl.contains_event`) and tabular row contents.\n325\t- All 5 roadmap→spec \"orphan\" IDs (`D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6`) are NOT present in `roadmap.md` as bare tokens at all. Only `D-5` and `D-8` (ADR labels) appear (`roadmap.md:57,86,109,344,348`). These are phantom matches from a buggy extraction regex, not real roadmap references.\n326\t\n327\tNet: 20/20 deviations are false-positives produced by the unimplemented classifier. Spec-fidelity is genuinely PASS.\n328\t\n329\t### Options\n330\t\n331\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n332\t|---|---|---|\n333\t| **A. Add `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"`; document manual-triage policy here; produce `deviation-triage.md`; resolve `remediation-tasklist.md` per disposition.** | Preserves audit trail. Distinguishes \"fidelity failed\" from \"classifier unimplemented, manual triage performed.\" Unblocks Phase 1 execution. | Requires per-deviation manual evidence (done in `deviation-triage.md`). |\n334\t| B. Flip `validation.status` to `\"pass\"` and delete the deviation records. | Cleanest. | Destroys audit trail of what the analyzer flagged and why the maintainer decided each was a false-positive. |\n335\t| C. Leave the label as `\"fail\"` and block Phase 1 until the classifier is implemented (backlog item `pipeline-classifier-implementation`). | Lets the pipeline self-heal eventually. | Stalls the cliEval release on infrastructure the release itself doesn't need. The classifier is a separate roadmap concern. |\n336\t\n337\t### Decision: **A — pass-with-triage label, manual-triage policy, per-deviation disposition.**\n338\t\n339\t### Rationale\n340\t\n341\t1. The pipeline's 14 step statuses already provide an objective PASS signal; the post-pipeline validator's `\"fail\"` label is one composite indicator that conflates two distinct conditions (fidelity failure vs. unclassified records pending triage). Splitting them removes ambiguity for future readers.\n342\t2. Option B destroys the only artifact (`spec-deviations.md` + `deviation-registry.json`) that would let a future classifier replay the same input and verify it produces the same dispositions. Triage is durable; deletion is not.\n343\t3. Option C blocks an unrelated release on a tooling backlog item with no committed timeline. The cliEval release does not depend on classifier implementation — it only depends on the dispositions being recorded.\n344\t4. Manual-triage policy fits within ADR-lite scope: per-deviation rows with file:line evidence in `deviation-triage.md` are auditable, and `remediation-tasklist.md` updates close the loop with the original BLOCKING entries.\n345\t\n346\t### Manual-Triage Policy\n347\t\n348\tWhen `validation.status` is `\"fail\"` purely due to unclassified-classifier output AND `fidelity_status` is `\"pass\"` AND every pipeline `steps.*` is PASS, a maintainer MAY perform manual triage by:\n349\t\n350\t1. Reading each finding in `spec-deviations.md` plus its registry entry in `deviation-registry.json`.\n351\t2. Cross-referencing the cited symbol/file/ID against `roadmap.md` and `decisions.md` to confirm presence or absence.\n352\t3. Assigning one of the following dispositions:\n353\t   - `NO_ACTION` — finding is a false-positive (analyzer extraction artifact or naming-only mismatch); cite the roadmap.md line that contains the symbol.\n354\t   - `MAPPED → <ROADMAP_ROW_ID>` — finding maps to an existing roadmap row; cite the row.\n355\t   - `ADD_TO_P1` / `ADD_NEW_TASK` — genuine gap; do NOT silently edit phase tasklists; surface for separate gated decision.\n356\t   - `BLOCKING-CONFIRMED` — actual fidelity failure; halts the release.\n357\t4. Recording per-deviation dispositions in `deviation-triage.md` with file:line evidence.\n358\t5. Updating `remediation-tasklist.md` entries (`[x]` + disposition + evidence) and flipping `[UNCLASSIFIED]` markers in `spec-deviations.md` to their resolved classification.\n359\t\n360\t### Consequences\n361\t\n362\t- `.roadmap-state.json` SHOULD gain `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"` once triage is complete. Per the user's instruction in this pre-execution-gate task, the JSON state file is NOT modified in this turn; the relabel is documented here as the authoritative semantic interpretation and is pending a follow-up edit.\n363\t- Future pipeline runs SHOULD distinguish \"fidelity FAIL\" from \"deviations pending triage\" via separate status fields rather than a single `validation.status` boolean.\n364\t- A backlog item should track implementing the deviation classifier so manual triage is no longer required (see `spec-deviations.md:10` reference to `pipeline-classifier-implementation`).\n365\t- The analyzer's roadmap→spec orphan ID extraction has a latent bug (phantom matches `D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6` that do not appear in roadmap.md); the classifier work should fix this regex.\n366\t- Phase-1 execution (`TASK-RF-20260518-cliEval-P1-pty-isolation-gates`) is UNBLOCKED upon triage completion.\n367\t\n368\t\n369\t---\n370\t\n371\t## D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution mechanism for vendored ptytest (OQ-4 closure)\n372\t\n373\t### Context\n374\t\n375\t`OQ-4` (roadmap.md:173) asks: *\"Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\"* Per debate convergence and roadmap row 132 (DOC-OQ4), OQ-4 is a hard M2 entry blocker — it must close before the vendored ptytest sources physically land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` (T02.01 / NFR-MAINT1). At the time this ADR lands, the repo has a top-level `LICENSE` (MIT) but no `NOTICE` file.\n376\t\n377\t`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is MIT-licensed. The MIT License requires that \"the above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.\" We are vendoring a substantial portion of ptytest sources, so the upstream copyright + permission notice must travel with the distribution.\n378\t\n379\t`design-spec.md:785-787` already names the intended mechanism: retain the upstream `LICENSE` verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`, and reference it from a top-level `NOTICE`. This ADR ratifies that mechanism and records it as the OQ-4 closure artifact.\n380\t\n381\t### Options\n382\t\n383\t| Option | Pros | Cons |\n384\t|---|---|---|\n385\t| **A. Top-level `NOTICE` referencing `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path; PROVENANCE.md records fork SHA + changes.** | Industry-standard pattern (matches Apache 2 / Google convention even though both licenses here are MIT). NOTICE is grep-able by license-scan tooling. Survives upstream skill/template churn because the NOTICE points at a path the project controls. | Adds one new top-level file. |\n386\t| B. Inline ptytest MIT terms into the repo root `LICENSE` as an appendix. | One fewer file. | Mixes IronClaude's own copyright with a third-party copyright in the same file — confusing for downstream license scanners, and the precedent generalizes badly when more third-party components arrive. |\n387\t| C. Rely solely on `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` without a top-level NOTICE. | Minimum disruption to existing tree. | Fails the convention that license tooling (e.g., FOSSA, REUSE, OSS Review Toolkit) scans the repo root for NOTICE-style attribution before recursing. Maintainer audit trail is weaker. |\n388\t\n389\t### Decision: **A — top-level `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path.**\n390\t\n391\t### Rationale\n392\t\n393\t1. The MIT obligation (\"retain the copyright notice and permission notice\") is satisfied by retaining the upstream LICENSE verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`. Adding a top-level `NOTICE` is **additive discipline** — it makes the attribution discoverable by repo-root scanners without dispersing the verbatim copyright across multiple files.\n394\t2. The `NOTICE` mechanism generalizes: when future third-party components are vendored (or when the harness adds an additional dependency that requires attribution), they get a new section in `NOTICE` and a verbatim LICENSE at their vendored path. Option B (inline appendix) does not generalize as cleanly.\n395\t3. Option C (no NOTICE) would force every reader and every license-scan tool to traverse the source tree to discover third-party content. The marginal cost of one top-level file is small; the marginal benefit (audit ergonomics) is real.\n396\t4. The `NOTICE` file does not change the license terms of either IronClaude or ptytest. Both remain MIT. `NOTICE` is documentation of attribution, not a license modification.\n397\t\n398\t### Closure of OQ-4\n399\t\n400\t- **Question:** Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\n401\t- **Resolution:** YES. A top-level `NOTICE` MUST exist before T02.01 lands vendored ptytest sources. The `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` (the verbatim upstream MIT terms) and `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` (fork SHA + changes).\n402\t- **Resolution status:** RESOLVED — 2026-05-20.\n403\t- **Resolution artifact:** `NOTICE` at repo root (this commit). The referenced `LICENSE` and `PROVENANCE.md` under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` land with T02.01 (NFR-MAINT1).\n404\t\n405\t### Attribution clause (canonical text)\n406\t\n407\tThe `NOTICE` file contains the following authoritative attribution clause for ptytest. Any future edit to this clause SHOULD update both the `NOTICE` file and this ADR section in lockstep:\n408\t\n409\t> **ptytest (vendored fork)** — Upstream: `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest`; License: MIT; Location: `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`. A fork of `brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is vendored under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` and used as the PTY/pexpect driver layer for the CLI evaluation harness. The upstream MIT LICENSE is retained verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`, and `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` records the fork SHA, vendoring date, and the changes made relative to upstream.\n410\t\n411\t### Consequences\n412\t\n413\t- A new file `NOTICE` exists at the repository root. It is documentation, not a build/test artifact; CI does not need to enforce its presence beyond a single grep check (`grep -c ptytest NOTICE >= 1`).\n414\t- T02.01 (NFR-MAINT1) is unblocked for M2 entry. The vendored ptytest sources may now physically land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`, with their own `LICENSE` (verbatim upstream) and `PROVENANCE.md` (fork SHA + changes).\n415\t- T02.03 (AC10 fork SHA pin + drift policy) inherits the resolution: the quarterly review cadence recorded in `PROVENANCE.md` keeps the `NOTICE` accurate over time. If a future resync changes the upstream copyright text, both `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` and the attribution clause above MUST be re-verified.\n416\t- Future vendored components MUST follow the same convention: section in `NOTICE` + verbatim LICENSE at the vendored path + PROVENANCE.md entry. Wire `--junit` into FR-CLI1.** Add the 12th flag; Reporter's `to_junit()` and `emit_junit` gate stand. Update spec §4 table to list `--junit`. | Matches the existing roadmap FR-CLI1 spec (R-072, 12 flags) and the Phase-4 T04.10 acceptance criterion (\"lists all 12 flags named in FR-CLI1\"). Reuses already-landed `to_junit()` (~50 LOC) without code removal. Keeps CI consumers' future path (JUnit-XML ingestion in CI runners is the de-facto pattern for pytest-style harnesses). | Adds one CLI flag and one feature-gated output file. |\n500\t| B. Remove `--junit` from spec §9 and `to_junit()` from §10. | Slightly smaller CLI surface; one fewer artifact path. | Contradicts the roadmap row R-072 (which would also need amendment), invalidates T04.10's \"12 flags\" AC (would need to be re-spun as \"11 flags\"), and forces re-removal of Reporter code that has already landed under T03.13. Option B would require amending both, plus removing the `to_junit()` line from spec §10 and the §9 junit.xml section. Option A requires updating only the §4 flag table.\n508\t3. **The `--junit` flag is feature-gated, not enabled by default.** `--junit` defaults to `false` and the Reporter only writes `junit.xml` when `emit_junit=True` (`reporter.py:222`). There is no runtime cost for runs that do not opt in, and the flag does not change the dimensional invariants of `summary.json` (the JUnit XML is rendered from the same `AggregatedRunReport`).\n509\t4. **JUnit XML is the dominant CI ingestion format.** GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Buildkite, Jenkins, and CircleCI all support JUnit XML test result publishing natively. All three artifacts now agree.\n511\t\n512\t### Closure of OQ-7\n513\t\n514\t- **Question:** Whether `--junit` flag is supported in CLI.\n515\t- **Resolution:** YES. See §OPS-001 Closure below; per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0021/spec.md`.\n- R4 (2026-05-20): DOC-OQ4 closure — D-10 added recording the NOTICE/LICENSE attribution mechanism for the vendored ptytest fork. OQ-4 status flips from OPEN to RESOLVED (M2 entry blocker for T02.01 cleared); per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0024/spec.md`.\n\n---\n\n## D-1: PTY layer — fork ptytest vs build minimal pexpect wrapper\n\n### Context\n\nReal Claude Code is a TTY-based interactive REPL. The eval harness needs a PTY (pseudo-terminal) driver that can spawn it, write to its stdin, read from its stdout/stderr, detect \"prompt ready\" states, kill on timeout, and capture exit codes.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons | LOC | Risk |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **A. Fork `brandon-fryslie/ptytest`** | Working Claude Code example already exists in repo. Plugin-free standalone class. Active enough (recent commits). | 1 star / 0 forks → bus-factor real. Includes pytest-fixture machinery we don't need. | ~300 LOC adaptation | LOW (ptytest is small, easy to own) |\n| B. Build minimal `pexpect` wrapper from scratch | Clean ownership, no upstream-drift risk, exactly the API we want. | More code to write, debug, test. We re-derive Claude Code TTY quirks. | ~700 LOC fresh | MEDIUM (pexpect has subtle gotchas) |\n| C. Use `pexpect` directly (no wrapper) | Zero abstraction overhead. | Every eval re-implements spawn/wait/expect_prompt patterns; code duplication. | ~0 LOC harness; +~50 LOC per eval | HIGH (drift across evals) |\n\n### Decision: **A — fork ptytest, vendor under `cli/eval/pty/`, fork-and-own posture.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The fork-research report verified ptytest has a working Claude Code example, which means the hardest TTY edge cases (prompt detection, ANSI escape handling, line-buffered reads) are already solved.\n2. Bus-factor risk is mitigated by **vendoring** (we copy the code in; no runtime pip dependency on the upstream repo). If upstream dies, we have a frozen working copy.\n3. The durable dependency is `pexpect` (2.8k stars, active again at v4.9) — ptytest is just a thin layer on top.\n4. 300 LOC adaptation is ~5x cheaper than 700 LOC fresh build.\n5. Option C (raw pexpect) fails the harness-cleanliness goal — evals should look like assertion code, not PTY plumbing.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- We own a small vendored fork forever. `PROVENANCE.md` documents the fork SHA and our diffs.\n- Quarterly review of upstream `pexpect` releases; resync if security-relevant.\n- A new file at `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` reproducing upstream MIT terms (license compliance).\n\n---\n\n## D-2: Assertion DSL — adopt `Expect.*` port vs raw assertions\n\n### Context\n\nEach eval needs to verify hook side-effects (file presence, JSONL events, settings.json registrations, exit codes, TTY output). The expression of these assertions can be:\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Port `mcp-eval`'s `Expect.tools.*` DSL idea as Python primitives** | Declarative YAML manifest possible. Reusable across evals. Single place to add new assertion types. Mental-model match with industry pattern. | +~200 LOC for the DSL itself. Some abstraction overhead (declarative → callable). |\n| B. Raw Python `assert` statements in each eval | Lowest abstraction. No new code. | Each eval is more code. Declarative YAML manifest not possible (or only partial). Failure messages are ad-hoc per eval. |\n| C. Use pytest's assertion machinery directly | Free; idiomatic. | Couples harness to pytest, but the harness is NOT pytest-driven (it's a CLI subcommand). |\n\n### Decision: **A — port `Expect.*` DSL, ~200 LOC, no upstream dependency.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The YAML manifest schema (§5 of design-spec) is a hard requirement to keep eval definitions declarative and reviewable. Raw Python assertions can't be expressed in YAML.\n2. A small DSL (~10 method types: `file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) covers all 15 evals comfortably.\n3. The mcp-eval API surface is a proven mental model — we steal the ergonomics without taking a dependency on the framework (which has the wrong architecture for our needs).\n4. Failure messages from a typed DSL are uniformly high-quality (the DSL knows its own structure); ad-hoc assertions tend to produce noisy error output.\n5. The DSL has a programmatic escape hatch for parameterized evals that can't be expressed in YAML — so we don't lock ourselves out of complex assertions.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `cli/eval/expect.py` is a public API surface; changes need backward-compat care once evals are written against it.\n- The YAML schema (`suites/suite.schema.json`) and the DSL must stay in lockstep — `loader.py` translates declarative → callable.\n- Out-of-scope for v1: LLM-judge assertions (mcp-eval has these; we don't need them for hook validation).\n\n---\n\n## D-3: HOME isolation — extend existing `IsolationLayers` vs separate class\n\n### Context\n\n`src/superclaude/cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182` already implements 4-layer per-subprocess isolation (`CLAUDE_WORK_DIR`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DIR`, `CLAUDE_SETTINGS_DIR`). The eval harness needs a 5th layer: `HOME` override (and `XDG_*` companions). Question: extend the existing class or build a new one?\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Compose `IsolationLayers` in a new `HomeIsolation` class** | Reuses 4 existing layers for free. No risk to sprint's existing usage. New `HOME` layer lives in eval code, not sprint code. | Two classes to maintain. Slight indirection. |\n| B. Add `HOME` layer to `IsolationLayers` directly (modify sprint code) | One unified class. Sprint also benefits from HOME isolation (theoretical future use). | Modifies code outside this release's scope. Sprint doesn't currently need HOME isolation, so YAGNI risk. Sprint regression risk. |\n| C. Build `HomeIsolation` standalone (no inheritance/composition) | Maximum decoupling. | Duplicate 4 layers of code. Bug-fixes diverge over time. |\n\n### Decision: **A — `HomeIsolation` COMPOSES `IsolationLayers` (has-a, not is-a).**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. Composition preserves sprint's existing class verbatim. Zero risk to PR #49-era infrastructure.\n2. The `HOME` layer is conceptually a \"5th layer,\" but its lifecycle (mkdtemp → install hooks → seed state → teardown) is more complex than the existing 4 (which are just env-var settings). Keeping it separate cleanly captures that asymmetry.\n3. If a future sprint feature ever needs HOME isolation, it can either re-use `HomeIsolation` directly or migrate the logic upward — easier path than splitting later.\n4. The interface in `design-spec.md` §7 already reflects this: `HomeIsolation` exposes `.env()` that returns the dict overlay, which the caller merges with `IsolationLayers.env()` output.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `HomeIsolation` constructor takes an `IsolationLayers` instance as a dependency.\n- Documentation in `cli/eval/isolation.py` cross-references `cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182`.\n- If the sprint `IsolationLayers` API ever changes shape, we have a single integration point to update.\n\n---\n\n## D-4: Eval registry — YAML manifest vs Python-decorator-registered\n\n### Context\n\nThe 15 evals need to be defined somewhere. The orchestrator needs to enumerate them, validate dependencies, schedule them, and report results. Options diverge on where the \"source of truth\" lives.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. YAML manifests under `suites/*.yaml`** | Declarative, human-readable, reviewable diffs, schema-validated, easy to add new suites (`fast.yaml`, `nightly.yaml`). Non-Python contributors can author/review evals. | Some Python required for complex pre/post logic; needs an \"escape hatch\" mechanism. |\n| B. Python-decorator-registered (`@eval(id=\"E1\", title=\"...\", requires=[...])`) | Maximum flexibility — eval bodies are first-class Python. Type-checking friendly. | Diff churn higher (Python > YAML in PR review noise). Adding new suites requires more boilerplate. Schema enforcement is via type hints, less ergonomic for non-Python contributors. |\n| C. Hybrid — YAML defines metadata, Python `@assertion_callback` for escape hatch | Best of both worlds for simple+complex evals. | More mechanism to learn; two source-of-truth surfaces. |\n\n### Decision: **A — YAML manifest as primary, with optional Python escape-hatch via `callback:` field for parameterized/complex evals.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. **Reviewability** is paramount: a PR adding a new eval should be readable as a YAML diff, not as a Python class with overrides. The 15-eval suite is meant to be a living spec — declarative beats imperative.\n2. The schema (§5 of design-spec) covers the common case (file/JSONL/settings/exit/stderr/duration assertions) elegantly.\n3. The `callback:` field provides escape-hatch power without forcing every eval through it:\n   ```yaml\n   - id: E14\n     title: \"concurrent SessionStart bursts\"\n     callback: superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start\n   ```\n   The callback is invoked only when an eval's logic can't be expressed in YAML (E14, possibly E15).\n4. New suites (`fast.yaml`, `regression.yaml`, etc.) drop in as data files with no code changes — exactly the kind of low-friction extension we want.\n5. `loader.py` validates against `suites/suite.schema.json` once; the rest of the pipeline trusts validated input.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `suites/suite.schema.json` is a public artifact (committed) — schema changes need versioning.\n- Callbacks live in `suites/<suite-name>_callbacks.py` co-located with the YAML.\n- Eval IDs (`E1`, `E2.1`, etc.) are stable identifiers — must not be renamed without a migration note.\n- Parameterized evals (e.g., E2's 3 prefix variants) use a `parameterize:` block in YAML; the loader expands these into separate `EvalSpec` instances at load time.\n\n---\n\n## D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate — falsifiable definition of \"equivalent regression\"\n\n### Context\n\nGoal G5 in design-spec §1 said \"catch the bug PR #49 fixed and any equivalent future regression\" — but \"equivalent\" was undefined. Spec-panel finding W-1 (CRITICAL) flagged this as unfalsifiable: with no measurable acceptance criterion, the harness has no way to answer \"did I do my job?\"\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Coverage gate: every matcher P in `hooks.json` must have ≥1 eval in the loaded suite** | Falsifiable, mechanically enforceable. CI-friendly. Maps to the actual class of bugs PR #49 lived in (matcher↔case-body drift). | Requires `eval doctor --check-coverage`. New eval must accompany every new hook matcher. |\n| B. Snapshot match — assert this commit's matchers equal a frozen golden list | Simple. | Frozen list goes stale; bug-class definition is \"matches a list\" not \"real-world hook fires.\" |\n| C. Leave G5 prose-only (\"intent is clear enough\") | Zero work. | Spec-panel CRITICAL stands; downstream tasklist has no test for G5. |\n\n### Decision: **A — coverage gate enforced by loader + `eval doctor --check-coverage`.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The class of bug PR #49 introduced was \"matcher exists in `hooks.json` but no real-world test exercises it.\" A coverage gate directly inverts that condition.\n2. Mechanical enforcement at load time means the harness fails fast (exit 2) when a new matcher lands without an eval — no silent drift.\n3. The gate runs in two places: (a) `eval doctor` for pre-flight, (b) top of `eval run` for the live run. Both are cheap (single pass over `hooks.json` + manifest).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `loader.py` reads `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` at run-start, extracts every PostToolUse matcher pattern, and verifies ≥1 eval in the manifest declares `inputs.expect_tool_call` matching each pattern.\n- New hook matcher PRs must include a paired eval — enforced by a CI lint (deferred per non-goals but the harness itself enforces locally).\n- Acceptable escape hatch: manifest declares `coverage_exempt_matchers: [\"<pattern>\"]` with a `reason:` field for matchers intentionally untestable.\n\n---\n\n## D-6: Disk-budget enforcement strategy\n\n### Context\n\nSpec-panel finding N-2 (CRITICAL) flagged internal inconsistency: §14 R4 cited `--max-disk-mb` as the mitigation but §4 flag table omitted the flag. Either add it or strike the mitigation.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Add `--max-disk-mb` flag (default 1024), poll every 5s, abort-on-breach** | Closes the cited risk. Bounded blast radius. Simple implementation. | One more flag. Polling adds tiny CPU overhead. |\n| B. Strike the mitigation from R4; document \"user must monitor disk manually\" | Zero code. | R4 becomes a known accepted risk; eval runs on a constrained box can fill disk. |\n| C. Hard limit via `setrlimit(RLIMIT_FSIZE)` on each subprocess | Kernel enforced. | Per-process limit, not cumulative; doesn't catch the 15-parallel-evals case. |\n\n### Decision: **A — add `--max-disk-mb` flag with 5-second polling.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The risk is real: 15 parallel evals × keep-home-on-failure × TTY transcripts can fill a small disk silently. A polling guard with graceful abort (in-flight evals complete, new ones blocked) preserves results-so-far.\n2. 5-second polling is cheap: one `du -sb` (or Python `os.walk` accumulator) per tick. At 15-parallel, the orchestrator is mostly blocked on pexpect anyway.\n3. Default of 1024 MB matches design-spec §12 sizing (~300 MB total artifacts at 15-eval max-keep). Generous but not unbounded.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- New flag `--max-disk-mb INT` in §4 flag table; default 1024; `0` disables.\n- New orchestrator component `DiskBudgetWatchdog` running in a sidecar thread.\n- New exit-2 reason code: `disk_budget_exceeded`.\n- Summary.json gains `harness.peak_disk_mb` field (also satisfies spec-panel H-1 partial).\n\n---\n\n## D-7: Path-traversal hardening for HomeIsolation\n\n### Context\n\nSpec-panel finding Wh-1 (CRITICAL) demonstrated that a malformed `eval_id` (e.g., `../../../etc/passwd` or `{session_id}` self-reference) could cause `HomeIsolation` to write outside the scratch root. The §14 R7 \"Hard guard\" was prose-only and ambiguous about whether `..` traversal was resolved before the containment check.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Defense-in-depth: loader regex + HomeIsolation `Path.resolve().is_relative_to()` + symlink-resolution after creation + scratch_root allowlist** | Catches the attack at three independent layers. Fails closed at every layer. | More code (~30 LOC). Three checks to maintain. |\n| B. Loader-only regex validation | Simple. | Single layer = single failure point. |\n| C. Trust the manifest author (no validation) | Zero work. | Manifest is data; data ingestion without validation is the original sin of basically every security incident. |\n\n### Decision: **A — three-layer defense-in-depth (regex + Path containment + symlink resolution + allowlist).**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The harness operates on user-supplied YAML and writes to disk. This is the textbook injection surface. Single-layer validation is industry-known-insufficient.\n2. Each layer catches a distinct attack class: regex blocks `..` and template tokens; `Path.resolve()` normalizes any survivor; `is_relative_to(scratch_root)` enforces containment; symlink resolution catches the \"scratch dir is a symlink to `$HOME`\" race; allowlist on `scratch_root` itself blocks `--output-dir /home/user/.claude`.\n3. Python 3.10+ (project minimum) provides `Path.is_relative_to()` natively.\n4. The performance cost is negligible (4 stat calls per eval setup).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `HomeIsolation.setup()` gains a documented pre-flight sequence (regex re-check → resolve → contain → symlink-resolve → write).\n- `EvalConfig` gains an `allowed_scratch_roots: tuple[Path, ...]` field, defaulting to `(/tmp/eval-runs, <repo>/.dev/eval-runs)`.\n- New exception type `HomeContainmentViolation` (subclass of `EvalHarnessError`); maps to status ERRORED.\n- Test `test_isolation.py` MUST include: (a) `..` traversal, (b) absolute-path eval_id, (c) symlink-to-home attack, (d) `--output-dir` outside allowlist.\n\n---\n\n## D-8: Reporter dimensional invariant (consumes N', not K)\n\n### Context\n\nSpec-panel Pipeline-CRITICAL showed §9's summary.json example reported `14 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped` (totals = 15, the full suite) but the spec text described capability filtering as removing evals from the schedule. If the reporter only sees the K-kept subset, a `--no-mcp` run that drops 5 evals would show \"10 passed\" with no audit trail of the 5 skipped — a silent regression in coverage measurement.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Orchestrator emits N' EvalOutcomes (one per expanded EvalSpec); SKIPPED carries reason; reporter asserts `len(outcomes) == N'`** | Audit trail preserved. Coverage queries answerable. Contract is checkable. | Slightly more state per skipped eval. |\n| B. Reporter takes K outcomes + a separate skipped-list; merges at render time | Same audit trail. | Two channels to keep in sync. More places to drop a skip. |\n| C. Status quo (K only, skip count is metadata) | Less code. | The spec-panel CRITICAL stands; this is the bug being filed. |\n\n### Decision: **A — single list of N' EvalOutcome records; reporter asserts dimensional invariant.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. A single dataclass list eliminates the \"two channels in sync\" failure mode of option B.\n2. The contract assertion (`len(outcomes) == counts.expanded_n_prime`) makes the invariant grep-able and test-able; CI can verify it doesn't regress.\n3. Status taxonomy expansion (8 statuses with explicit FAIL/ERRORED/TIMEOUT/INTERRUPTED/SKIPPED/XFAIL/XPASS distinction) is a natural consequence — addresses the spec-panel \"failure-mode taxonomy is sparse\" consensus point.\n4. Performance cost is zero in practice (the SKIPPED records are empty-shell; N' is at most 17 with parameterize expansion).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- New dataclass `EvalOutcome` in `models.py` (see design-spec §9 \"Aggregator data contract\").\n- New status values `ERRORED`, `INTERRUPTED`, `XFAIL`, `XPASS` (in addition to PASS/FAIL/TIMEOUT/SKIPPED) — taxonomy frozen in design-spec §9.\n- New error `ReporterContractViolation` raised on dimensional mismatch at `AggregatedRunReport.from_outcomes()`.\n- Summary.json gains `counts` block (manifest_n, expanded_n_prime, kept_k, skipped_s).\n- Exit-code mapping in §4 cross-references this taxonomy.\n\n---\n\n## Cross-cutting decision: implementation order\n\n(Not a strict ADR, but worth recording.) Per §17 of `design-spec.md`, the implementation phases are:\n\n1. **Phase 1** (1 day): vendored `pty/`, `HomeIsolation`, capability gates, `eval doctor`. Smoke: `superclaude eval doctor` green.\n2. **Phase 2** (1 day): `loader.py`, `models.py`, `expect.py`, `eval describe/list`. Smoke: parse a 1-eval manifest.\n3. **Phase 3** (1 day): `orchestrator.py`, `runner.py`, `reporter.py`, `eval run`. Smoke: 1 real Claude Code session driven end-to-end.\n4. **Phase 4** (0.5 day): wire into `cli/main.py`, Makefile target, .gitignore. Smoke: `make verify-sync` still EXIT=0.\n5. **Phase 5** (1-2 weeks): implement E1-E15 in 3-5 PR batches.\n\n**Rationale for this ordering:** Phase 1 is the riskiest (PTY mechanics). Validating the harness can spawn-and-observe ONE Claude Code session is the gate that determines whether the entire approach is viable. If Phase 1 fails (e.g., Claude Code's TTY behavior breaks pexpect-style detection), we re-pivot before committing to the rest of the architecture.\n\n---\n\n## Sign-off\n\n| Decision | Status | Signed | Date |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| D-1: PTY layer = fork ptytest | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n| D-2: Assertion DSL = Expect.* port | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n| D-3: HOME isolation = compose IsolationLayers | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n| D-4: Eval registry = YAML + callback escape | 🟡 PROPOSED | — | — |\n| D-5: Hook-matcher coverage gate (G5 falsifiable) | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-6: `--max-disk-mb` poller (R4 enforcement) | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-7: Three-layer path-traversal hardening | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-8: Reporter consumes N' + status taxonomy | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R3) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n| D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution for vendored ptytest | 🟠 QUEUED FOR SIGN-OFF (R4) | — | 2026-05-20 |\n\n**Maintainer:** mark each row 🟢 APPROVED + sign + date before invoking `/sc:roadmap`.\n\n**Reject/revise:** add a row to a \"Revisions\" section below; do not edit the original decisions in-place.\n\n---\n\n## Revisions\n\n(none yet)\n\n---\n\n## D-9: Reconcile `validation.status: \"fail\"` label and document manual-triage policy for unclassified deviations\n\n### Context\n\n`.roadmap-state.json:113-117` reports `fidelity_status: \"pass\"` and `validation.status: \"fail\"` (timestamp 2026-05-18T19:47:24Z, six minutes after the final pipeline step completed). However, every pipeline step in `steps.*` — including `spec-fidelity`, `wiring-verification`, `deviation-analysis`, and `remediate` — recorded `status: \"PASS\"`. `spec-fidelity.md:1-7` likewise records 0 HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW deviations and PASS convergence.\n\nThe `validation.status: \"fail\"` label was stamped by a separate validator step that reacts to the 20 records in `spec-deviations.md`. Per `spec-deviations.md:10`, \"deviation classification is not yet implemented. All records currently render as UNCLASSIFIED.\" `remediation-tasklist.md:14-34` then escalates all 20 to `BLOCKING` with owner `UNKNOWN` purely because the classifier never ran — not because spec-fidelity actually regressed.\n\nA manual cross-check (performed during this reconciliation) confirms:\n\n- All 15 spec→roadmap \"orphan\" findings (4 files + 11 `Expect.*` predicate helpers) ARE present in `roadmap.md` — the files appear at `roadmap.md:102,142,409-412` and the 11 helpers are enumerated in row `COMP-010` at `roadmap.md:77`. The analyzer's bare-identifier string match missed qualified references (e.g., `Expect.jsonl.contains_event`) and tabular row contents.\n- All 5 roadmap→spec \"orphan\" IDs (`D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6`) are NOT present in `roadmap.md` as bare tokens at all. Only `D-5` and `D-8` (ADR labels) appear (`roadmap.md:57,86,109,344,348`). These are phantom matches from a buggy extraction regex, not real roadmap references.\n\nNet: 20/20 deviations are false-positives produced by the unimplemented classifier. Spec-fidelity is genuinely PASS.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Add `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"`; document manual-triage policy here; produce `deviation-triage.md`; resolve `remediation-tasklist.md` per disposition.** | Preserves audit trail. Distinguishes \"fidelity failed\" from \"classifier unimplemented, manual triage performed.\" Unblocks Phase 1 execution. | Requires per-deviation manual evidence (done in `deviation-triage.md`). |\n| B. Flip `validation.status` to `\"pass\"` and delete the deviation records. | Cleanest. | Destroys audit trail of what the analyzer flagged and why the maintainer decided each was a false-positive. |\n| C. Leave the label as `\"fail\"` and block Phase 1 until the classifier is implemented (backlog item `pipeline-classifier-implementation`). | Lets the pipeline self-heal eventually. | Stalls the cliEval release on infrastructure the release itself doesn't need. The classifier is a separate roadmap concern. |\n\n### Decision: **A — pass-with-triage label, manual-triage policy, per-deviation disposition.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The pipeline's 14 step statuses already provide an objective PASS signal; the post-pipeline validator's `\"fail\"` label is one composite indicator that conflates two distinct conditions (fidelity failure vs. unclassified records pending triage). Splitting them removes ambiguity for future readers.\n2. Option B destroys the only artifact (`spec-deviations.md` + `deviation-registry.json`) that would let a future classifier replay the same input and verify it produces the same dispositions. Triage is durable; deletion is not.\n3. Option C blocks an unrelated release on a tooling backlog item with no committed timeline. The cliEval release does not depend on classifier implementation — it only depends on the dispositions being recorded.\n4. Manual-triage policy fits within ADR-lite scope: per-deviation rows with file:line evidence in `deviation-triage.md` are auditable, and `remediation-tasklist.md` updates close the loop with the original BLOCKING entries.\n\n### Manual-Triage Policy\n\nWhen `validation.status` is `\"fail\"` purely due to unclassified-classifier output AND `fidelity_status` is `\"pass\"` AND every pipeline `steps.*` is PASS, a maintainer MAY perform manual triage by:\n\n1. Reading each finding in `spec-deviations.md` plus its registry entry in `deviation-registry.json`.\n2. Cross-referencing the cited symbol/file/ID against `roadmap.md` and `decisions.md` to confirm presence or absence.\n3. Assigning one of the following dispositions:\n   - `NO_ACTION` — finding is a false-positive (analyzer extraction artifact or naming-only mismatch); cite the roadmap.md line that contains the symbol.\n   - `MAPPED → <ROADMAP_ROW_ID>` — finding maps to an existing roadmap row; cite the row.\n   - `ADD_TO_P1` / `ADD_NEW_TASK` — genuine gap; do NOT silently edit phase tasklists; surface for separate gated decision.\n   - `BLOCKING-CONFIRMED` — actual fidelity failure; halts the release.\n4. Recording per-deviation dispositions in `deviation-triage.md` with file:line evidence.\n5. Updating `remediation-tasklist.md` entries (`[x]` + disposition + evidence) and flipping `[UNCLASSIFIED]` markers in `spec-deviations.md` to their resolved classification.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `.roadmap-state.json` SHOULD gain `validation.unclassified_deviations: 20` and `validation.status: \"pass-with-triage\"` once triage is complete. Per the user's instruction in this pre-execution-gate task, the JSON state file is NOT modified in this turn; the relabel is documented here as the authoritative semantic interpretation and is pending a follow-up edit.\n- Future pipeline runs SHOULD distinguish \"fidelity FAIL\" from \"deviations pending triage\" via separate status fields rather than a single `validation.status` boolean.\n- A backlog item should track implementing the deviation classifier so manual triage is no longer required (see `spec-deviations.md:10` reference to `pipeline-classifier-implementation`).\n- The analyzer's roadmap→spec orphan ID extraction has a latent bug (phantom matches `D-1`, `D12`, `D3`, `D5`, `D6` that do not appear in roadmap.md); the classifier work should fix this regex.\n- Phase-1 execution (`TASK-RF-20260518-cliEval-P1-pty-isolation-gates`) is UNBLOCKED upon triage completion.\n\n\n---\n\n## D-10: NOTICE/LICENSE attribution mechanism for vendored ptytest (OQ-4 closure)\n\n### Context\n\n`OQ-4` (roadmap.md:173) asks: *\"Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\"* Per debate convergence and roadmap row 132 (DOC-OQ4), OQ-4 is a hard M2 entry blocker — it must close before the vendored ptytest sources physically land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` (T02.01 / NFR-MAINT1). At the time this ADR lands, the repo has a top-level `LICENSE` (MIT) but no `NOTICE` file.\n\n`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is MIT-licensed. The MIT License requires that \"the above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.\" We are vendoring a substantial portion of ptytest sources, so the upstream copyright + permission notice must travel with the distribution.\n\n`design-spec.md:785-787` already names the intended mechanism: retain the upstream `LICENSE` verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`, and reference it from a top-level `NOTICE`. This ADR ratifies that mechanism and records it as the OQ-4 closure artifact.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Top-level `NOTICE` referencing `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path; PROVENANCE.md records fork SHA + changes.** | Industry-standard pattern (matches Apache 2 / Google convention even though both licenses here are MIT). NOTICE is grep-able by license-scan tooling. Survives upstream skill/template churn because the NOTICE points at a path the project controls. | Adds one new top-level file. |\n| B. Inline ptytest MIT terms into the repo root `LICENSE` as an appendix. | One fewer file. | Mixes IronClaude's own copyright with a third-party copyright in the same file — confusing for downstream license scanners, and the precedent generalizes badly when more third-party components arrive. |\n| C. Rely solely on `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` without a top-level NOTICE. | Minimum disruption to existing tree. | Fails the convention that license tooling (e.g., FOSSA, REUSE, OSS Review Toolkit) scans the repo root for NOTICE-style attribution before recursing. Maintainer audit trail is weaker. |\n\n### Decision: **A — top-level `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`; upstream LICENSE retained verbatim at the vendored path.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The MIT obligation (\"retain the copyright notice and permission notice\") is satisfied by retaining the upstream LICENSE verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`. Adding a top-level `NOTICE` is **additive discipline** — it makes the attribution discoverable by repo-root scanners without dispersing the verbatim copyright across multiple files.\n2. The `NOTICE` mechanism generalizes: when future third-party components are vendored (or when the harness adds an additional dependency that requires attribution), they get a new section in `NOTICE` and a verbatim LICENSE at their vendored path. Option B (inline appendix) does not generalize as cleanly.\n3. Option C (no NOTICE) would force every reader and every license-scan tool to traverse the source tree to discover third-party content. The marginal cost of one top-level file is small; the marginal benefit (audit ergonomics) is real.\n4. The `NOTICE` file does not change the license terms of either IronClaude or ptytest. Both remain MIT. `NOTICE` is documentation of attribution, not a license modification.\n\n### Closure of OQ-4\n\n- **Question:** Does the repo require a top-level NOTICE file for ptytest attribution?\n- **Resolution:** YES. A top-level `NOTICE` MUST exist before T02.01 lands vendored ptytest sources. The `NOTICE` references `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` (the verbatim upstream MIT terms) and `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` (fork SHA + changes).\n- **Resolution status:** RESOLVED — 2026-05-20.\n- **Resolution artifact:** `NOTICE` at repo root (this commit). The referenced `LICENSE` and `PROVENANCE.md` under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` land with T02.01 (NFR-MAINT1).\n\n### Attribution clause (canonical text)\n\nThe `NOTICE` file contains the following authoritative attribution clause for ptytest. Any future edit to this clause SHOULD update both the `NOTICE` file and this ADR section in lockstep:\n\n> **ptytest (vendored fork)** — Upstream: `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest`; License: MIT; Location: `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`. A fork of `brandon-fryslie/ptytest` is vendored under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` and used as the PTY/pexpect driver layer for the CLI evaluation harness. The upstream MIT LICENSE is retained verbatim at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`, and `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` records the fork SHA, vendoring date, and the changes made relative to upstream.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- A new file `NOTICE` exists at the repository root. It is documentation, not a build/test artifact; CI does not need to enforce its presence beyond a single grep check (`grep -c ptytest NOTICE >= 1`).\n- T02.01 (NFR-MAINT1) is unblocked for M2 entry. The vendored ptytest sources may now physically land under `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`, with their own `LICENSE` (verbatim upstream) and `PROVENANCE.md` (fork SHA + changes).\n- T02.03 (AC10 fork SHA pin + drift policy) inherits the resolution: the quarterly review cadence recorded in `PROVENANCE.md` keeps the `NOTICE` accurate over time. If a future resync changes the upstream copyright text, both `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` and the attribution clause above MUST be re-verified.\n- Future vendored components MUST follow the same convention: section in `NOTICE` + verbatim LICENSE at the vendored path + PROVENANCE.md entry. The spec carried conditional language in two non-adjacent places: §9 (\"`junit.xml` ... Generated only when `--junit` is passed\") and §10 (\"Add `to_json()` and `to_junit()` (new methods)\"), while the §4 flag table omitted `--junit` from the 11-flag list. The roadmap FR-CLI1 row (R-072) and the Phase-4 task T04.10 both name a 12-flag set that includes `--junit`. The drift between §4 and FR-CLI1 is the surface OQ-7 was opened to close.\n\nDOC-OQ7 (R-076) names two outcomes: (a) wire `--junit` into FR-CLI1 with `junit.xml` emission, OR (b) remove the conditional `--junit` language from spec §9. Either resolves the drift; the choice is binding for M4 exit.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Description | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **A. Wire `--junit` into FR-CLI1.** Add the 12th flag; Reporter's `to_junit()` and `emit_junit` gate stand. Update spec §4 table to list `--junit`. | Matches the existing roadmap FR-CLI1 spec (R-072, 12 flags) and the Phase-4 T04.10 acceptance criterion (\"lists all 12 flags named in FR-CLI1\"). Reuses already-landed `to_junit()` (~50 LOC) without code removal. Keeps CI consumers' future path (JUnit-XML ingestion in CI runners is the de-facto pattern for pytest-style harnesses). | Adds one CLI flag and one feature-gated output file. |\n| B. Remove `--junit` from spec §9 and `to_junit()` from §10. | Slightly smaller CLI surface; one fewer artifact path. | Contradicts the roadmap row R-072 (which would also need amendment), invalidates T04.10's \"12 flags\" AC (would need to be re-spun as \"11 flags\"), and forces re-removal of Reporter code that has already landed under T03.13. The cost is documentation churn in 3 places plus code deletion for net-zero capability change. |\n\n### Decision: **A — wire `--junit` into FR-CLI1; spec §9 conditional language is correct as written; spec §4 flag table updated to include `--junit` for consistency.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. **The implementation has already converged on option A.** The Reporter's `to_junit()` method and `emit_junit` constructor flag landed under T03.13 (`src/superclaude/cli/eval/reporter.py:146,177,222-225`); the CLI's `--junit` flag landed under T04.10 (`src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py:1349-1352,1366,1593`). Choosing option B now would require deleting working code to match a spec that no other artifact endorses.\n2. **The roadmap and tasklist already commit to 12 flags.** R-072 acceptance criterion enumerates 12 flag names including `--junit`; T04.10 acceptance criterion says \"all 12 flags named in FR-CLI1\". Option B would require amending both, plus removing the `to_junit()` line from spec §10 and the §9 junit.xml section. Option A requires updating only the §4 flag table.\n3. **The `--junit` flag is feature-gated, not enabled by default.** `--junit` defaults to `false` and the Reporter only writes `junit.xml` when `emit_junit=True` (`reporter.py:222`). There is no runtime cost for runs that do not opt in, and the flag does not change the dimensional invariants of `summary.json` (the JUnit XML is rendered from the same `AggregatedRunReport`).\n4. **JUnit XML is the dominant CI ingestion format.** GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Buildkite, Jenkins, and CircleCI all support JUnit XML test result publishing natively. The marginal cost of a ~50 LOC `to_junit()` renderer plus one Click option is small; the marginal benefit (zero-config CI publishing without a separate adapter) is real and is the use case spec §4 example #4 already shows.\n5. **Risk R1 (\"Flag drift between spec §4 and implementation\") is closed by this decision.** §4 table is updated to list `--junit`; FR-CLI1 (R-072) already lists it; implementation already wires it. All three artifacts now agree.\n\n### Closure of OQ-7\n\n- **Question:** Whether `--junit` flag is supported in CLI.\n- **Resolution:** YES. The CLI supports `superclaude eval run --junit` as a feature-gated flag (default `false`); when set, `Reporter.write()` emits `junit.xml` into the run output directory alongside `summary.md` / `summary.json`.\n- **Resolution status:** RESOLVED — 2026-05-20.\n- **Resolution artifact:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py:1349-1593` (CLI flag wiring) + `src/superclaude/cli/eval/reporter.py:146-225` (Reporter feature gate and renderer).\n\n### Consequences\n\n- Spec §4 flag table is updated in this commit to list `--junit BOOL` (default `false`) alongside the other 11 flags. The §4 table now agrees with FR-CLI1 (R-072) and T04.10 on the 12-flag set.\n- Spec §9 conditional language (\"Generated only when `--junit` is passed\") stands as written. Spec §10 `to_junit()` reference stands.\n- T04.10 (FR-CLI1 wiring) is satisfied for the `--junit` row; T04.13 (FR-G4 artifact layout) includes `junit.xml` only when `emit_junit=True`.\n- TEST-007 reporter-contract suite (T04.17) covers `to_junit()` invariants implicitly via the schema-fidelity test; a future test refinement may add a JUnit-specific assertion (out of scope for T04.15).\n- The OPS-001 Open-Questions table (§B above) flips OQ-7 from `OPEN` to `RESOLVED — 2026-05-20`. The table itself is not re-rendered here; the resolution status above is canonical and supersedes the prior `OPEN` row by date.\n- No code change accompanies this ADR closure beyond the §4 table update; the implementation was already in place.\n\n---\n\n## OQ-2 Resolution — E3..E15 eval body shapes frozen (T05.01)\n\n**Source:** Roadmap row 110 (OQ-2) — *\"Concrete content of E3–E15 manifest entries.\"*\n**Task:** T05.01 (Phase 5, Clarification)\n**Deliverable:** D-0082 — see `artifacts/D-0082/spec.md` for the full per-eval body table; Purpose\n\nOQ-2 (roadmap.md:110) asks: *\"Concrete content of E3–E15 manifest entries.\"*\nThe design-spec (§5) shows only E1 and E2 parameterize as exemplars; E3–E15 are deferred.\n\nThis spec freezes the **inputs, expects, and capability tags** for each of the 13 remaining bodies so that T05.07 … T05.21 may author them without further design churn.\n\n## 2. Design constraints\n\n1. **Coverage:** every hook event type in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` MUST be exercised by ≥1 eval among {E1, E2.1–3, E3 … E15}.\n2. **Determinism:** every body MUST pass deterministically on a clean per-eval HOME (FR-ISO2) — no time-of-day, no network, no shared mutable state. (3-run determinism is the per-task AC.)\n3. **No OQ-8 dependency:** E3–E15 MUST NOT require `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`. The original design-spec note tying E3 to \"30-min freshness tests\" is **superseded**; freshness-staleness via time offset is deferred until OQ-8 closes (separate follow-up).\n4. **Expect.* primitive only:** every body's assertions MUST be expressible via the v1 DSL (`file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) plus the YAML `callback:` escape (D-4) for E14.\n5. **Capability gating:** evals requiring a live MCP server soft-skip under `--no-mcp` with `skip_reason` populated (FR-CAP1).\n\n## 3. (b) the `pre_edit` substring pins that the\n79\tPreToolUse hook **emitted its event row**; (c) the `Edit` substring\n80\tpins that the **Edit branch specifically** fired (not Write / not\n81\tserena); (d) the scratch file persistence pins that the Edit operation\n82\tcompleted; (e) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\n83\tshutdown.\n84\t\n85\tThe two-substring shape (`pre_edit` + `Edit`) mirrors the E2.1-3\n86\tmatcher-coverage triad pattern (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape\n87\t({event, tool})\") — separating the *event-fired* assertion from the\n88\t*specific-matcher-fired* assertion so a regression in matcher routing\n89\tfails on the second substring even if the first still passes.\n90\t\n91\tThe D-0082 §4 second assertion `event_count == 1` (matcher-pin singular\n92\tEdit tool call) is functionally covered by combining the substring\n93\tassertion with the eval's input shape (exactly one Edit prompt in\n94\t`inputs[]`); the precise per-input count predicate requires a Python\n95\tcallable and is deferred — see §3 footnote on YAML expressibility.\n96\t\n97\t## 3.
