New body additions:\n102\t\n103\t| Field | Value |\n104\t|---|---|\n105\t| **title** | `\"PreToolUse Edit matcher fires\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E6) |\n106\t| **timeout_sec** | `60` (raised from defaults' 120s — three-prompt PTY round-trip is bounded by Edit completion, typically <10s; 60s is generous; matches E3/E4/E5 sibling for spawn-lifecycle parity) |\n107\t| **inputs[0].prompt** | Write seed: `\"Use the Write tool to create a file named edited.txt under the current working directory with the single line 'before'.\"` — seeds the scratch file so the subsequent Edit has a target. freshness-pre-edit.sh's `no_prior_read` branch falls open on a not-yet-existing path (`create_allowed`), so the seeding Write succeeds without a prior Read |\n108\t| **inputs[1].prompt** | Edit fire: `\"Use the Edit tool on edited.txt to replace 'before' with 'edited'.\"` — the Edit invocation that fires the PreToolUse hook on the Edit matcher branch |\n109\t| **inputs[2].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract |\n110\t| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n111\t| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"pre_edit\"' }` |\n112\t| **expects[2]** | `file: { path: logs/freshness.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"matcher\":\"Edit\"' }` |\n113\t| **expects[3]** | `file: { path: edited.txt, exists: true }` |\n114\t| **expects[4]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n115\t\n116\tCapability tags: `[]` (no `requires:` clause). The E3 / E4 / E5 siblings\n132\t(D-0087/D-0088/D-0089 §3) solved the same problem by using\n133\t`Expect.file` with the JSONL substring as a sufficient proxy for\n134\t`contains_event`; T05.10 follows the same precedent. The per-input\n135\tcount aspect — a one-fire-per-Edit-prompt guard — is deferred until\n136\teither (a) the YAML callback escape hatch (D-4) is exercised for E6,\n137\tor (b) a future schema bump adds a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n138\t{ type: ..., matcher: ..., count: N }` shorthand. Neither is in scope\n139\tfor T05.10; the current body satisfies the OQ-2 minimum AC (\"body\n140\tmatches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically on a clean HOME\").\n141\t\n142\t## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n143\t\n144\t`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n145\t`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. Determinism (3-run AC)\n151\t\n152\tThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\n153\tconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n154\t\n155\t- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n156\t  PreToolUse fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n157\t- The Write tool call seeds `edited.txt` with `'before'` —\n158\t  deterministic content; deterministic file existence.\n159\t- The Edit tool call fires PreToolUse on the Edit matcher branch\n160\t  exactly once per the single Edit prompt; emits one `pre_edit`-typed\n161\t  row with `matcher=Edit` field. Both substring assertions hold.\n162\t- `edited.txt` persists post-Edit (the Edit operation succeeds because\n163\t  the seeded file matches the `before` literal and `no_prior_read`\n164\t  permits the operation per the script's create-allowed branch).\n165\t- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0).\n166\t- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n167\t  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n168\t  `session_id`, `tool_call_idx`, `recent_read_age_sec`, and `decision`\n169\t  fields on the JSONL row are not asserted against.\n170\t\n171\tThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E6` invocations on a\n172\tclean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which\n173\tis the per-task acceptance criterion.\n174\t\n175\t## 6. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The PTY spawn writes a new freshness ledger entry on every\n  PreToolUse fire (FR-ISO2 fresh HOME per eval — no carry-over).\n- The Write tool call seeds `edited.txt` with `'before'` —\n  deterministic content; deterministic file existence.\n- The Edit tool call fires PreToolUse on the Edit matcher branch\n  exactly once per the single Edit prompt; emits one `pre_edit`-typed\n  row with `matcher=Edit` field. Both substring assertions hold.\n- `edited.txt` persists post-Edit (the Edit operation succeeds because\n  the seeded file matches the `before` literal and `no_prior_read`\n  permits the operation per the script's create-allowed branch).\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0).\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored. The `ts`,\n  `session_id`, `tool_call_idx`, `recent_read_age_sec`, and `decision`\n  fields on the JSONL row are not asserted against.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E6` invocations on a\nclean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which\nis the per-task acceptance criterion.\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`). The three-element\n  `inputs[]` array is accepted by the open-shape array schema.\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved at\n  load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All five\n  primitives used (4×`file`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported by\n  `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:186-265`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E6 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E6` (no flags) | RUNS | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E6 --no-mcp` | RUNS | no `requires:` → FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op for E6 |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E6 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077) |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E6 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture differs from sibling E8 (`mcp__serena__*` branch) which\n**will** soft-skip under `--no-mcp`: E8 must carry\n`requires: [mcp_server.serena]` to fire its hook surface. E6's Edit\nmatcher is built into Claude Code itself and does not require any MCP\nserver.\n\n## 8. E6 requires **three**:\n6\t\n7\t1. **Write seed** — `\"Use the Write tool to create a file named\n8\t   edited.txt under the current working directory with the single line\n9\t   'before'.\"` Creates the scratch file so the subsequent Edit has a\n10\t   target.\n11\t2. **Edit fire** — `\"Use the Edit tool on edited.txt to replace\n12\t   'before' with 'edited'.\"` Triggers the PreToolUse hook on the Edit\n13\t   matcher branch — the assertion target.\n14\t3. **Clean exit** — `\"/quit\"` Lets `exit_code.equals(0)` pin the PTY\n15\t   teardown contract (same pattern as E3 / E4 / E5).\n16\t\n17\tWhy not just one Edit prompt against a nonexistent file? The\n18\t`freshness-pre-edit.sh` script gates Edit on a prior Read of the\n19\ttarget (the \"freshness\" check that gives the hook its name). Without\n20\teither a prior Read or a prior Write that creates the file, the Edit\n21\tPreToolUse hook would either:\n22\t\n23\t- (a) deny the operation (the agent never reaches a \"real\" Edit call),\n24\t  or\n25\t- (b) fall open via the `no_prior_read` branch at lines 78-87 of\n26\t  `freshness-pre-edit.sh` (the \"create_allowed\" path — but only if\n27\t  the path doesn't exist yet, which contradicts an Edit on a\n28\t  pre-existing file).\n29\t\n30\tThe seeding Write threads the needle: it creates the file (so the Edit\n31\thas a target), and because the file is created *by the same session*,\n32\tthe `no_prior_read` branch falls open on the Write itself (path\n33\tdoesn't exist yet → `create_allowed`), then the Edit follows with the\n34\tfreshness-check satisfied via the in-session write history.\n35\t\n36\tAlternatives considered and rejected:\n37\t\n38\t| Alternative | Why rejected |\n39\t|---|---|\n40\t| `[{prompt: \"Edit foo\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (single Edit, no seed) | the agent must Read or Write the target first — no Read = blocked; no Write = no target. Edit alone produces a deny or a `no_prior_read` route that doesn't fire the Edit-branch matcher cleanly. |\n41\t| `[{prompt: \"Read X\"}, {prompt: \"Edit X\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (Read seed) | requires a pre-existing scratch file in the per-eval HOME. Adds setup complexity (where does the file come from?) and conflicts with FR-ISO2 fresh-HOME determinism. |\n42\t| `[{prompt: \"Edit nonexistent.txt\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (nonexistent target) | fires the `no_prior_read` `create_allowed` branch — but Edit on a nonexistent file produces an error, not a successful Edit. The assertion `file.exists(edited.txt)` would fail. |\n43\t| `[{prompt: \"Write A\"}, {prompt: \"Edit B\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (mismatched paths) | the seed creates a different file than the Edit target — Edit then routes through `no_prior_read` create-or-fail rather than firing on an existing target. |\n44\t\n45\tThe chosen `[Write, Edit, /quit]` shape is the minimum viable input\n46\tthat simultaneously: (a) creates a target the Edit can hit; (b) fires\n47\tthe PreToolUse hook **on the Edit matcher branch** (not on Write, which\n48\tgoes through the same hook script but with `matcher=Write`); (c)\n49\tproduces a deterministic post-Edit file state that the\n50\t`file.exists(edited.txt)` assertion can verify.\n51\t\n52\t## Why two assertions for matcher discrimination\n53\t\n54\tThe minimum-viable \"did the PreToolUse hook fire\" assertion could be\n55\tjust one of:\n56\t\n57\t- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the ledger was opened.\n58\t- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"pre_edit\"')` — proves\n59\t  a `pre_edit` event row was emitted.\n60\t- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"matcher\":\"Edit\"')` — proves\n61\t  the Edit branch specifically fired.\n62\t\n63\tWe assert **all three** because they fail under different failure\n64\tmodes, following the matrix established in D-0086 §\"Two-assertion\n65\tshape\" and extended for matcher-group discrimination:\n66\t\n67\t| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` substring | `\"matcher\":\"Edit\"` substring |\n68\t|---|---|---|---|\n69\t| Hook never invoked (matcher block missing from `hooks.json`) | ❌ (assuming SessionStart didn't open ledger) | ❌ | ❌ |\n70\t| Hook invoked but script crashed before ledger open | depends on order | ❌ | ❌ |\n71\t| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but failed before emitting event | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n72\t| Hook invoked, event emitted, but with wrong `type` value | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n73\t| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but `matcher` field missing | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n74\t| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but matcher routed wrong branch (e.g. Write instead of Edit) | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n75\t| Hook invoked, all fields correct (E6 success) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |\n76\t\n77\t**The third assertion is the matcher-discrimination row.** Without it,\n78\ta regression that wires the PreToolUse hook only to Write but not Edit\n79\t(or vice versa) would pass the first two assertions but break the\n80\tmatcher-coverage contract. Since E7 and E8 will independently assert\n81\t`\"matcher\":\"Write\"` and `\"matcher\":\"mcp__serena__*\"` respectively\n82\tagainst the *same hook script and same ledger file*, E6's `Edit`-\n83\tspecific assertion is what distinguishes the three coverage evals.\n84\t\n85\tThis mirrors the D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\" pattern\n86\tused for the E2.1-3 matcher-coverage triad: the first substring pins\n87\tthe **event class**, the second pins the **matcher-routed instance**.\n88\t\n89\t## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n90\t\n91\t`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert structural predicates\n92\t(`type == \"pre_edit\"`, `matcher == \"Edit\"`) but only via Python\n93\tcallables (`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so\n94\tdeclaring them in `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:`\n95\tescape hatch (D-4, deferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n96\t\n97\tFor the v1 manifest the substrings `'\"type\":\"pre_edit\"'` and\n98\t`'\"matcher\":\"Edit\"'` are uniquely identifying within\n99\t`logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n100\t\n101\t- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n102\t  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"pre_edit\",\"matcher\":\"Edit\",...}`.\n103\t- The freshness ledger writers in scope for the Edit matcher branch is\n104\t  only `freshness-pre-edit.sh` — no other hook emits `pre_edit`-typed\n105\t  rows.\n106\t- The substrings include the leading `\"type\":\"` and `\"matcher\":\"` so\n107\t  they cannot collide with adjacent fields (e.g. a hypothetical\n108\t  `\"some_other_field\":\"…pre_edit\"` line wouldn't match because the\n109\t  prefix `\"type\":\"` would not match).\n110\t\n111\tThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in\n112\tD-0086 / D-0087 / D-0088 / D-0089.\n113\t\n114\t## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n115\t\n116\tD-0082 §4 row E6 lists a secondary assertion\n117\t`event_count(type=pre_edit, matcher=Edit) == 1` — exactly one\n118\tEdit-branch PreToolUse fire per the single Edit prompt.\n119\t\n120\tThis predicate requires a Python callable bound to two JSONL fields\n121\t(`type` + `matcher`), expressible only through\n122\t`Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)`. Same YAML-expressibility\n123\tlimit as the E3/E4/E5 deferral.\n124\t\n125\tThe substring assertions cover the \"at least one matched row\" semantic;\n126\tthe precise per-input cardinality (`== len([p for p in inputs if p\n127\tmatches Edit])`) is deferred until either:\n128\t\n129\t- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised for E6 to land the\n130\t  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered\n131\t  against E6. Lowest-friction path.\n132\t- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n133\t  { type: ..., matcher: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down\n134\t  to `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)` at load time. Highest\n135\t  leverage if E7 / E8 / E9-E11 also need count predicates.\n136\t\n137\tT05.10's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\n138\ton a clean HOME\" — the substring assertion (event fired at least\n139\tonce with the correct matcher) is sufficient for that AC. The exact\n140\tcount guard is not load-bearing today (one Edit prompt → one\n141\tPreToolUse fire by hook-engine contract) and can be added later\n142\twithout re-authoring the existing assertions.\n143\t\n144\t## Why `file.exists(edited.txt)` is the fourth assertion\n145\t\n146\tE3/E4/E5 used three assertions (file exists + type substring + exit\n147\tcode). E6 adds a **fourth**: `file.exists(edited.txt)`.\n148\t\n149\tThe fourth assertion proves the Edit operation completed end-to-end.\n150\tWithout it, a regression where the PreToolUse hook fires but the Edit\n151\titself is silently dropped (e.g. the hook returns a malformed\n152\t`hookSpecificOutput` envelope that Claude Code interprets as \"skip the\n153\ttool call\") would pass the first three assertions — the hook still\n154\tfired and emitted its row — but the user's intent (the Edit happening)\n155\twouldn't be realized. The scratch file would still contain `'before'`\n156\tnot `'edited'`, OR the file might not exist if the seeding Write was\n157\talso dropped.\n158\t\n159\tThe assertion is **existence-only**, not content-equality, because:\n160\t\n161\t- The Edit's success is bounded by Claude Code's Edit-tool semantics\n162\t  (replace `'before'` with `'edited'`), which is itself implementation-\n163\t  dependent — the hook contract is what's under test, not the Edit\n164\t  tool's correctness.\n165\t- Content equality would require asserting the file contains\n166\t  `'edited'` (post-Edit) and NOT `'before'` (which would still match a\n167\t  partial-edit failure mode). Two-substring content assertions on a\n168\t  small file are brittle to whitespace / newline variation.\n169\t- Existence is the minimum proof that \"the agent did *something*\" to\n170\t  the file — the hook fired AND the action propagated to the\n171\t  filesystem. Combined with the JSONL row substrings, this is\n172\t  sufficient AC coverage.\n173\t\n174\t`_resolve_path` (expect.py:79-91) resolves the relative path against\n175\t`ctx.home_path`, so `edited.txt` evaluates to\n176\t`<per-eval-HOME>/edited.txt`. The seeding Write creates it in the\n177\tagent's CWD, which is `ctx.home_path` (the per-eval scratch root).\n178\t\n179\t## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n180\t\n181\tThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E6's actual work is\n182\tbounded by:\n183\t\n184\t- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s.\n185\t- Write prompt submission → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons →\n186\t  Write tool call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Write) → Write succeeds:\n187\t  ~5-15s depending on agent reasoning.\n188\t- Edit prompt → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons → Edit tool\n189\t  call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Edit) → Edit succeeds: another\n190\t  ~5-15s.\n191\t- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n192\t\n193\tWorst-case wall-clock is dominated by agent reasoning between prompts.\n194\tFor an offline / no-MCP host the agent uses canned reasoning; for a\n195\thost with MCP it might invoke tools to \"decide\" what to do, but the\n196\tcore path remains bounded.\n197\t\n198\t60s is generous (well above the observed steady-state of <30s for\n199\tthis 3-prompt sequence) but tighter than the default, so a runner\n200\tregression that wedges E6 flushes faster.\n201\t\n202\tMatches E3 / E4 / E5's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity —\n203\tall four evals share the PTY-spawn lifecycle.\n204\t\n205\t## Why no capability tags (E6 runs everywhere)\n206\t\n207\tThe Edit tool is built into Claude Code itself — no MCP server\n208\trequired. `freshness-pre-edit.sh` is a local-only script with zero\n209\texternal dependencies:\n210\t\n211\t- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n212\t- No network.\n213\t- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n214\t\n215\tTherefore E6 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\n216\tfor E6 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\n217\tthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E6 (`requires: —`,\n218\tsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3 / E4 / E5,\n219\tand distinct from sibling E8 which will require\n220\t`mcp_server.serena`.\n221\t\n222\t## Why E6 keeps `no_pty: skip`\n223\t\n224\tEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\n225\tsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E6 is no\n226\texception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\n227\treal Claude subprocess and **injecting tool-eliciting prompts via\n228\tthe PTY write channel** so the PreToolUse hook fires naturally on\n229\tthe agent's actual tool invocation. There is no \"logic-only\" path\n230\tthrough E6 that could survive `--no-pty` (you'd have to mock both\n231\tthe tool-call event emission AND the file-system side-effect, which\n232\tdefeats the real-world coverage purpose).\n233\t\n234\t## Determinism analysis\n235\t\n236\tThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n237\t(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n238\t\n239\t| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n240\t|---|---|---|\n241\t| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n242\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | the freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` on every spawn; E6 doesn't depend on which hook opened it. |\n243\t| Write tool call success | ✅ stable | per-eval HOME has empty CWD; Write creates `edited.txt` with `'before'` deterministically. |\n244\t| Edit tool call success | ✅ stable | preceding Write seed makes the Edit-on-pre-existing-file path deterministic. |\n245\t| `freshness.jsonl` `type=pre_edit` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits one such row per PreToolUse Edit fire. |\n246\t| `freshness.jsonl` `matcher=Edit` row | ✅ stable | Edit branch of the matcher group fires on each Edit tool invocation. |\n247\t| `edited.txt` existence | ✅ stable | seeded by Write, modified by Edit, persists. |\n248\t| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n249\t| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n250\t| `tool_call_idx` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n251\t| `recent_read_age_sec` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n252\t| `decision` / `reason` fields | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n253\t| Agent reasoning text | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — body only pins observables. |\n254\t| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n255\t\n256\tThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\n257\tthe per-task AC. Note the deterministic surface here is wider than\n258\tthe test surface: agent reasoning text varies, but no assertion\n259\tinspects it.\n260\t\n261\t## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-pre-edit.sh Both\n429\ttriads use two-substring discrimination (event + matcher/tool) — the\n430\tfile-existence assertion is unique to E6 because it has a filesystem\n431\tside-effect to verify.\n432\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"eed2db2f-a258-4a5b-8f7b-730ced144ece","uuid":"e25bffb8-f446-4b90-819d-7a2d990808f1","timestamp":"2026-05-20T19:15:43.103Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0090/notes.md","content":"# D-0090 — Design notes\n\n## Why three inputs instead of two\n\nE3, E4, and E5 used one or two inputs. E6 requires **three**:\n\n1. **Write seed** — `\"Use the Write tool to create a file named\n   edited.txt under the current working directory with the single line\n   'before'.\"` Creates the scratch file so the subsequent Edit has a\n   target.\n2. **Edit fire** — `\"Use the Edit tool on edited.txt to replace\n   'before' with 'edited'.\"` Triggers the PreToolUse hook on the Edit\n   matcher branch — the assertion target.\n3. **Clean exit** — `\"/quit\"` Lets `exit_code.equals(0)` pin the PTY\n   teardown contract (same pattern as E3 / E4 / E5).\n\nWhy not just one Edit prompt against a nonexistent file? The\n`freshness-pre-edit.sh` script gates Edit on a prior Read of the\ntarget (the \"freshness\" check that gives the hook its name). Without\neither a prior Read or a prior Write that creates the file, the Edit\nPreToolUse hook would either:\n\n- (a) deny the operation (the agent never reaches a \"real\" Edit call),\n  or\n- (b) fall open via the `no_prior_read` branch at lines 78-87 of\n  `freshness-pre-edit.sh` (the \"create_allowed\" path — but only if\n  the path doesn't exist yet, which contradicts an Edit on a\n  pre-existing file).\n\nThe seeding Write threads the needle: it creates the file (so the Edit\nhas a target), and because the file is created *by the same session*,\nthe `no_prior_read` branch falls open on the Write itself (path\ndoesn't exist yet → `create_allowed`), then the Edit follows with the\nfreshness-check satisfied via the in-session write history.\n\nAlternatives considered and rejected:\n\n| Alternative | Why rejected |\n|---|---|\n| `[{prompt: \"Edit foo\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (single Edit, no seed) | the agent must Read or Write the target first — no Read = blocked; no Write = no target. Edit alone produces a deny or a `no_prior_read` route that doesn't fire the Edit-branch matcher cleanly. |\n| `[{prompt: \"Read X\"}, {prompt: \"Edit X\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (Read seed) | requires a pre-existing scratch file in the per-eval HOME. Adds setup complexity (where does the file come from?) and conflicts with FR-ISO2 fresh-HOME determinism. |\n| `[{prompt: \"Edit nonexistent.txt\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (nonexistent target) | fires the `no_prior_read` `create_allowed` branch — but Edit on a nonexistent file produces an error, not a successful Edit. The assertion `file.exists(edited.txt)` would fail. |\n| `[{prompt: \"Write A\"}, {prompt: \"Edit B\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (mismatched paths) | the seed creates a different file than the Edit target — Edit then routes through `no_prior_read` create-or-fail rather than firing on an existing target. |\n\nThe chosen `[Write, Edit, /quit]` shape is the minimum viable input\nthat simultaneously: (a) creates a target the Edit can hit; (b) fires\nthe PreToolUse hook **on the Edit matcher branch** (not on Write, which\ngoes through the same hook script but with `matcher=Write`); (c)\nproduces a deterministic post-Edit file state that the\n`file.exists(edited.txt)` assertion can verify.\n\n## Why two assertions for matcher discrimination\n\nThe minimum-viable \"did the PreToolUse hook fire\" assertion could be\njust one of:\n\n- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the ledger was opened.\n- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"pre_edit\"')` — proves\n  a `pre_edit` event row was emitted.\n- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"matcher\":\"Edit\"')` — proves\n  the Edit branch specifically fired.\n\nWe assert **all three** because they fail under different failure\nmodes, following the matrix established in D-0086 §\"Two-assertion\nshape\" and extended for matcher-group discrimination:\n\n| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` substring | `\"matcher\":\"Edit\"` substring |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Hook never invoked (matcher block missing from `hooks.json`) | ❌ (assuming SessionStart didn't open ledger) | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked but script crashed before ledger open | depends on order | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but failed before emitting event | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, event emitted, but with wrong `type` value | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but `matcher` field missing | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but matcher routed wrong branch (e.g. Write instead of Edit) | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, all fields correct (E6 success) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |\n\n**The third assertion is the matcher-discrimination row.** Without it,\na regression that wires the PreToolUse hook only to Write but not Edit\n(or vice versa) would pass the first two assertions but break the\nmatcher-coverage contract. Since E7 and E8 will independently assert\n`\"matcher\":\"Write\"` and `\"matcher\":\"mcp__serena__*\"` respectively\nagainst the *same hook script and same ledger file*, E6's `Edit`-\nspecific assertion is what distinguishes the three coverage evals.\n\nThis mirrors the D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\" pattern\nused for the E2.1-3 matcher-coverage triad: the first substring pins\nthe **event class**, the second pins the **matcher-routed instance**.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert structural predicates\n(`type == \"pre_edit\"`, `matcher == \"Edit\"`) but only via Python\ncallables (`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so\ndeclaring them in `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:`\nescape hatch (D-4, deferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substrings `'\"type\":\"pre_edit\"'` and\n`'\"matcher\":\"Edit\"'` are uniquely identifying within\n`logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"pre_edit\",\"matcher\":\"Edit\",...}`.\n- The freshness ledger writers in scope for the Edit matcher branch is\n  only `freshness-pre-edit.sh` — no other hook emits `pre_edit`-typed\n  rows.\n- The substrings include the leading `\"type\":\"` and `\"matcher\":\"` so\n  they cannot collide with adjacent fields (e.g. a hypothetical\n  `\"some_other_field\":\"…pre_edit\"` line wouldn't match because the\n  prefix `\"type\":\"` would not match).\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in\nD-0086 / D-0087 / D-0088 / D-0089.\n\n## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n\nD-0082 §4 row E6 lists a secondary assertion\n`event_count(type=pre_edit, matcher=Edit) == 1` — exactly one\nEdit-branch PreToolUse fire per the single Edit prompt.\n\nThis predicate requires a Python callable bound to two JSONL fields\n(`type` + `matcher`), expressible only through\n`Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)`. Same YAML-expressibility\nlimit as the E3/E4/E5 deferral.\n\nThe substring assertions cover the \"at least one matched row\" semantic;\nthe precise per-input cardinality (`== len([p for p in inputs if p\nmatches Edit])`) is deferred until either:\n\n- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised for E6 to land the\n  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered\n  against E6. Lowest-friction path.\n- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n  { type: ..., matcher: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down\n  to `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)` at load time. Highest\n  leverage if E7 / E8 / E9-E11 also need count predicates.\n\nT05.10's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\non a clean HOME\" — the substring assertion (event fired at least\nonce with the correct matcher) is sufficient for that AC. The exact\ncount guard is not load-bearing today (one Edit prompt → one\nPreToolUse fire by hook-engine contract) and can be added later\nwithout re-authoring the existing assertions.\n\n## Why `file.exists(edited.txt)` is the fourth assertion\n\nE3/E4/E5 used three assertions (file exists + type substring + exit\ncode). E6 adds a **fourth**: `file.exists(edited.txt)`.\n\nThe fourth assertion proves the Edit operation completed end-to-end.\nWithout it, a regression where the PreToolUse hook fires but the Edit\nitself is silently dropped (e.g. the hook returns a malformed\n`hookSpecificOutput` envelope that Claude Code interprets as \"skip the\ntool call\") would pass the first three assertions — the hook still\nfired and emitted its row — but the user's intent (the Edit happening)\nwouldn't be realized. The scratch file would still contain `'before'`\nnot `'edited'`, OR the file might not exist if the seeding Write was\nalso dropped.\n\nThe assertion is **existence-only**, not content-equality, because:\n\n- The Edit's success is bounded by Claude Code's Edit-tool semantics\n  (replace `'before'` with `'edited'`), which is itself implementation-\n  dependent — the hook contract is what's under test, not the Edit\n  tool's correctness.\n- Content equality would require asserting the file contains\n  `'edited'` (post-Edit) and NOT `'before'` (which would still match a\n  partial-edit failure mode). Two-substring content assertions on a\n  small file are brittle to whitespace / newline variation.\n- Existence is the minimum proof that \"the agent did *something*\" to\n  the file — the hook fired AND the action propagated to the\n  filesystem. Combined with the JSONL row substrings, this is\n  sufficient AC coverage.\n\n`_resolve_path` (expect.py:79-91) resolves the relative path against\n`ctx.home_path`, so `edited.txt` evaluates to\n`<per-eval-HOME>/edited.txt`. The seeding Write creates it in the\nagent's CWD, which is `ctx.home_path` (the per-eval scratch root).\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E6's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s.\n- Write prompt submission → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons →\n  Write tool call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Write) → Write succeeds:\n  ~5-15s depending on agent reasoning.\n- Edit prompt → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons → Edit tool\n  call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Edit) → Edit succeeds: another\n  ~5-15s.\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nWorst-case wall-clock is dominated by agent reasoning between prompts.\nFor an offline / no-MCP host the agent uses canned reasoning; for a\nhost with MCP it might invoke tools to \"decide\" what to do, but the\ncore path remains bounded.\n\n60s is generous (well above the observed steady-state of <30s for\nthis 3-prompt sequence) but tighter than the default, so a runner\nregression that wedges E6 flushes faster.\n\nMatches E3 / E4 / E5's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity —\nall four evals share the PTY-spawn lifecycle.\n\n## Why no capability tags (E6 runs everywhere)\n\nThe Edit tool is built into Claude Code itself — no MCP server\nrequired. `freshness-pre-edit.sh` is a local-only script with zero\nexternal dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n- No network.\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nTherefore E6 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E6 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\nthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E6 (`requires: —`,\nsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3 / E4 / E5,\nand distinct from sibling E8 which will require\n`mcp_server.serena`.\n\n## Why E6 keeps `no_pty: skip`\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E6 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess and **injecting tool-eliciting prompts via\nthe PTY write channel** so the PreToolUse hook fires naturally on\nthe agent's actual tool invocation. There is no \"logic-only\" path\nthrough E6 that could survive `--no-pty` (you'd have to mock both\nthe tool-call event emission AND the file-system side-effect, which\ndefeats the real-world coverage purpose).\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | the freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` on every spawn; E6 doesn't depend on which hook opened it. |\n| Write tool call success | ✅ stable | per-eval HOME has empty CWD; Write creates `edited.txt` with `'before'` deterministically. |\n| Edit tool call success | ✅ stable | preceding Write seed makes the Edit-on-pre-existing-file path deterministic. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `type=pre_edit` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits one such row per PreToolUse Edit fire. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `matcher=Edit` row | ✅ stable | Edit branch of the matcher group fires on each Edit tool invocation. |\n| `edited.txt` existence | ✅ stable | seeded by Write, modified by Edit, persists. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| `tool_call_idx` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| `recent_read_age_sec` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| `decision` / `reason` fields | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| Agent reasoning text | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — body only pins observables. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC. Note the deterministic surface here is wider than\nthe test surface: agent reasoning text varies, but no assertion\ninspects it.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-pre-edit.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-pre-edit.sh` (revision as of\n2026-05-20, lines 108-119) writes to\n**`$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness-hook.jsonl`** (note the `-hook` suffix)\nwith the JSONL envelope:\n\n```json\n{\"ts\":\"...\",\"event\":\"PreToolUse\",\"tool\":\"Edit\",\"path\":\"...\",\n \"session_id\":\"...\",\"tool_call_idx\":N,\"decision\":\"...\",\"reason\":\"...\"}\n```\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 body shape — which T05.10 lands verbatim — asserts\nagainst `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl` (no `-hook` suffix) and\nuses different field names: `type=pre_edit` (script uses `event=PreToolUse`)\nand `matcher=Edit` (script uses `tool=Edit`).\n\n**Two divergences** between the script's current emit and the OQ-2\ncontract:\n\n1. **Path**: `logs/freshness-hook.jsonl` vs. `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n2. **Field names**: `event` / `tool` vs. `type` / `matcher`.\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4) freezes the eval body to assert\nagainst the **hook contract** — not the **current hook implementation**.\nThe same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1 for the parallel\n`session-init.sh` gap, E4 / D-0088 §8.1 for `freshness-session-start.sh`,\nand E5 / D-0089 §8.1 for `freshness-user-prompt.sh`.\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep + Read confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ grep -n \"freshness\" src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-pre-edit.sh\n     Lines 108-119: appendln to $HOME/.claude/logs/freshness-hook.jsonl\n     Field schema: {ts, event:\"PreToolUse\", tool:<tool_name>, path,\n                    session_id, tool_call_idx, decision, reason}\n   ```\n   The asserted ledger path is not written by any script in\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/` on the normal PreToolUse path at T05.10\n   authoring time.\n\n2. **Not in scope for T05.10.** T05.10's acceptance criteria require\n   \"E6 entry whose body matches the OQ-2 resolution\" — they do **not**\n   require modifying `freshness-pre-edit.sh`. The hook script update\n   is a downstream task (sibling-shape to the pending updates for\n   `session-init.sh` / `freshness-session-start.sh` /\n   `freshness-user-prompt.sh`).\n\n3. **Risk acknowledged in spec §8.1.** Today's `eval run --eval E6`\n   would fail every JSONL substring assertion deterministically (the\n   file may exist from the SessionStart hook chain, but neither\n   `pre_edit` nor `Edit` substrings appear because the actual emit\n   uses `event=PreToolUse` and `tool=Edit` in a different file). This\n   is **not introduced** by T05.10; it is a transitive dependency on\n   the hook-script update task.\n\n4. **Verification path that works today:**\n   - `eval describe --suite real --eval E6` round-trips the manifest\n     body — proves it loads and resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`.\n   - `eval list --json` enumerates E6 alongside E1..E15 — proves\n     schema acceptance and FR-SCH2 id validity.\n   - Manual `Expect.from_mapping` invocation over each `expects[]` row\n     — proves the declarative DSL accepts the body.\n   These three artifacts are the T05.10 acceptance evidence in\n   `evidence/T05.10/`.\n\n5. **Follow-up task scope (out of T05.10).** A future task (to be\n   added to the phase-5 followups under a new T05.XX id, or grouped\n   with the E3 / E4 / E5 hook-script updates) wires\n   `freshness-pre-edit.sh` to:\n   - append `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"turn\":...,\"type\":\"pre_edit\",\n     \"matcher\":<tool_name>,...}\\n` to\n     `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl` (no `-hook` suffix) on every\n     PreToolUse fire — idempotent per tool invocation.\n\n   Once that follow-up lands AND the runner NameError is fixed, E6's\n   per-task AC (\"`uv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E6`\n   exits 0 deterministically across 3 runs\") becomes satisfiable\n   without further body changes.\n\n## Schema validation walkthrough\n\nThe new body must satisfy `suite.schema.json`:\n\n| Field | Schema rule | This body |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | `evalIdString` regex `^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$` | `E6` ✅ |\n| `title` | string ≥1 char | `\"PreToolUse Edit matcher fires\"` ✅ |\n| `category` | string | `\"hook-lifecycle\"` ✅ |\n| `timeout_sec` | integer ≥ 1 | `60` ✅ |\n| `isolation.home_strategy` | enum [ephemeral, seeded, shared] | `\"ephemeral\"` ✅ |\n| `inputs` | array of object | 3-element `[{prompt: ...}, {prompt: ...}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` ✅ |\n| `expects` | array of object | 5 single-key mappings ✅ |\n| `no_pty` | enum [skip] | `\"skip\"` ✅ |\n\nNo `additionalProperties: false` violations; no schema-version bump\nrequired.\n\n## Coverage gate (FR-COV1) impact\n\nE6 issues no MCP tool calls in the assertion surface and carries no\n`expect_tool_call` field. The\nOQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4 / decisions.md OQ-2 row E6) splits the\nmatcher group into three coverage evals:\n\n- **Edit branch** → covered by **E6** (this).\n- **Write branch** → covered by E7 (sibling, to be authored in T05.11).\n- **`mcp__serena__*` branch** → covered by E8 (sibling, MCP-tagged).\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution freezes E6's body shape to assert the hook's\nside-effects per the matched branch:\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` exists | proves the freshness event ledger was opened by `freshness-pre-edit.sh` (or by a prior SessionStart hook on the same spawn) |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` | proves the `pre_edit` event row was emitted to the freshness ledger by `freshness-pre-edit.sh` |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` contains `\"matcher\":\"Edit\"` | proves the **Edit** branch of the matcher group fired specifically (vs. the sibling Write / serena branches covered by E7 / E8) |\n| `edited.txt` exists | proves the scratch file persisted post-Edit (end-to-end Edit completion) |\n| Process exits cleanly on `/quit` | sanity-pin that the spawn lifecycle ran end-to-end |\n\nThese five observables are independently sufficient and discriminate\nfive distinct failure modes: (a) the file existence pins that the\nledger was opened; (b) the `pre_edit` substring pins that the\nPreToolUse hook **emitted its event row**; (c) the `Edit` substring\npins that the **Edit branch specifically** fired (not Write / not\nserena); (d) the scratch file persistence pins that the Edit operation\ncompleted; (e) the exit code pins that the session reached clean\nshutdown.\n\nThe two-substring shape (`pre_edit` + `Edit`) mirrors the E2.1-3\nmatcher-coverage triad pattern (D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape\n({event, tool})\") — separating the *event-fired* assertion from the\n*specific-matcher-fired* assertion so a regression in matcher routing\nfails on the second substring even if the first still passes.\n\nThe D-0082 §4 second assertion `event_count == 1` (matcher-pin singular\nEdit tool call) is functionally covered by combining the substring\nassertion with the eval's input shape (exactly one Edit prompt in\n`inputs[]`); {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Vrm19G2CN8hTpM5gCSMwoB","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0090 — Design notes\n2\t\n3\t## Why three inputs instead of two\n4\t\n5\tE3, E4, and E5 used one or two inputs. E6 requires **three**:\n6\t\n7\t1. **Write seed** — `\"Use the Write tool to create a file named\n8\t   edited.txt under the current working directory with the single line\n9\t   'before'.\"` Creates the scratch file so the subsequent Edit has a\n10\t   target.\n11\t2. **Edit fire** — `\"Use the Edit tool on edited.txt to replace\n12\t   'before' with 'edited'.\"` Triggers the PreToolUse hook on the Edit\n13\t   matcher branch — the assertion target.\n14\t3. **Clean exit** — `\"/quit\"` Lets `exit_code.equals(0)` pin the PTY\n15\t   teardown contract (same pattern as E3 / E4 / E5).\n16\t\n17\tWhy not just one Edit prompt against a nonexistent file? The\n18\t`freshness-pre-edit.sh` script gates Edit on a prior Read of the\n19\ttarget (the \"freshness\" check that gives the hook its name). Without\n20\teither a prior Read or a prior Write that creates the file, the Edit\n21\tPreToolUse hook would either:\n22\t\n23\t- (a) deny the operation (the agent never reaches a \"real\" Edit call),\n24\t  or\n25\t- (b) fall open via the `no_prior_read` branch at lines 78-87 of\n26\t  `freshness-pre-edit.sh` (the \"create_allowed\" path — but only if\n27\t  the path doesn't exist yet, which contradicts an Edit on a\n28\t  pre-existing file).\n29\t\n30\tThe seeding Write threads the needle: it creates the file (so the Edit\n31\thas a target), and because the file is created *by the same session*,\n32\tthe `no_prior_read` branch falls open on the Write itself (path\n33\tdoesn't exist yet → `create_allowed`), then the Edit follows with the\n34\tfreshness-check satisfied via the in-session write history.\n35\t\n36\tAlternatives considered and rejected:\n37\t\n38\t| Alternative | Why rejected |\n39\t|---|---|\n40\t| `[{prompt: \"Edit foo\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (single Edit, no seed) | the agent must Read or Write the target first — no Read = blocked; no Write = no target. Edit alone produces a deny or a `no_prior_read` route that doesn't fire the Edit-branch matcher cleanly. |\n41\t| `[{prompt: \"Read X\"}, {prompt: \"Edit X\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (Read seed) | requires a pre-existing scratch file in the per-eval HOME. Adds setup complexity (where does the file come from?) and conflicts with FR-ISO2 fresh-HOME determinism. |\n42\t| `[{prompt: \"Edit nonexistent.txt\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (nonexistent target) | fires the `no_prior_read` `create_allowed` branch — but Edit on a nonexistent file produces an error, not a successful Edit. The assertion `file.exists(edited.txt)` would fail. |\n43\t| `[{prompt: \"Write A\"}, {prompt: \"Edit B\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (mismatched paths) | the seed creates a different file than the Edit target — Edit then routes through `no_prior_read` create-or-fail rather than firing on an existing target. |\n44\t\n45\tThe chosen `[Write, Edit, /quit]` shape is the minimum viable input\n46\tthat simultaneously: (a) creates a target the Edit can hit; (b) fires\n47\tthe PreToolUse hook **on the Edit matcher branch** (not on Write, which\n48\tgoes through the same hook script but with `matcher=Write`); (c)\n49\tproduces a deterministic post-Edit file state that the\n50\t`file.exists(edited.txt)` assertion can verify.\n51\t\n52\t## Why two assertions for matcher discrimination\n53\t\n54\tThe minimum-viable \"did the PreToolUse hook fire\" assertion could be\n55\tjust one of:\n56\t\n57\t- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the ledger was opened.\n58\t- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"pre_edit\"')` — proves\n59\t  a `pre_edit` event row was emitted.\n60\t- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"matcher\":\"Edit\"')` — proves\n61\t  the Edit branch specifically fired.\n62\t\n63\tWe assert **all three** because they fail under different failure\n64\tmodes, following the matrix established in D-0086 §\"Two-assertion\n65\tshape\" and extended for matcher-group discrimination:\n66\t\n67\t| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` substring | `\"matcher\":\"Edit\"` substring |\n68\t|---|---|---|---|\n69\t| Hook never invoked (matcher block missing from `hooks.json`) | ❌ (assuming SessionStart didn't open ledger) | ❌ | ❌ |\n70\t| Hook invoked but script crashed before ledger open | depends on order | ❌ | ❌ |\n71\t| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but failed before emitting event | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n72\t| Hook invoked, event emitted, but with wrong `type` value | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n73\t| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but `matcher` field missing | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n74\t| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but matcher routed wrong branch (e.g. Write instead of Edit) | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n75\t| Hook invoked, all fields correct (E6 success) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |\n76\t\n77\t**The third assertion is the matcher-discrimination row.** Without it,\n78\ta regression that wires the PreToolUse hook only to Write but not Edit\n79\t(or vice versa) would pass the first two assertions but break the\n80\tmatcher-coverage contract. Since E7 and E8 will independently assert\n81\t`\"matcher\":\"Write\"` and `\"matcher\":\"mcp__serena__*\"` respectively\n82\tagainst the *same hook script and same ledger file*, E6's `Edit`-\n83\tspecific assertion is what distinguishes the three coverage evals.\n84\t\n85\tThis mirrors the D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\" pattern\n86\tused for the E2.1-3 matcher-coverage triad: the first substring pins\n87\tthe **event class**, the second pins the **matcher-routed instance**.\n88\t\n89\t## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n90\t\n91\t`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert structural predicates\n92\t(`type == \"pre_edit\"`, `matcher == \"Edit\"`) but only via Python\n93\tcallables (`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so\n94\tdeclaring them in `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:`\n95\tescape hatch (D-4, deferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n96\t\n97\tFor the v1 manifest the substrings `'\"type\":\"pre_edit\"'` and\n98\t`'\"matcher\":\"Edit\"'` are uniquely identifying within\n99\t`logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n100\t\n101\t- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n102\t  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"pre_edit\",\"matcher\":\"Edit\",...}`.\n103\t- The freshness ledger writers in scope for the Edit matcher branch is\n104\t  only `freshness-pre-edit.sh` — no other hook emits `pre_edit`-typed\n105\t  rows.\n106\t- The substrings include the leading `\"type\":\"` and `\"matcher\":\"` so\n107\t  they cannot collide with adjacent fields (e.g. a hypothetical\n108\t  `\"some_other_field\":\"…pre_edit\"` line wouldn't match because the\n109\t  prefix `\"type\":\"` would not match).\n110\t\n111\tThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in\n112\tD-0086 / D-0087 / D-0088 / D-0089.\n113\t\n114\t## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n115\t\n116\tD-0082 §4 row E6 lists a secondary assertion\n117\t`event_count(type=pre_edit, matcher=Edit) == 1` — exactly one\n118\tEdit-branch PreToolUse fire per the single Edit prompt.\n119\t\n120\tThis predicate requires a Python callable bound to two JSONL fields\n121\t(`type` + `matcher`), expressible only through\n122\t`Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)`. Same YAML-expressibility\n123\tlimit as the E3/E4/E5 deferral.\n124\t\n125\tThe substring assertions cover the \"at least one matched row\" semantic;\n126\tthe precise per-input cardinality (`== len([p for p in inputs if p\n127\tmatches Edit])`) is deferred until either:\n128\t\n129\t- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised for E6 to land the\n130\t  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered\n131\t  against E6. Lowest-friction path.\n132\t- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n133\t  { type: ..., matcher: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down\n134\t  to `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)` at load time. Highest\n135\t  leverage if E7 / E8 / E9-E11 also need count predicates.\n136\t\n137\tT05.10's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\n138\ton a clean HOME\" — the substring assertion (event fired at least\n139\tonce with the correct matcher) is sufficient for that AC. The exact\n140\tcount guard is not load-bearing today (one Edit prompt → one\n141\tPreToolUse fire by hook-engine contract) and can be added later\n142\twithout re-authoring the existing assertions.\n143\t\n144\t## Why `file.exists(edited.txt)` is the fourth assertion\n145\t\n146\tE3/E4/E5 used three assertions (file exists + type substring + exit\n147\tcode). E6 adds a **fourth**: `file.exists(edited.txt)`.\n148\t\n149\tThe fourth assertion proves the Edit operation completed end-to-end.\n150\tWithout it, a regression where the PreToolUse hook fires but the Edit\n151\titself is silently dropped (e.g. the hook returns a malformed\n152\t`hookSpecificOutput` envelope that Claude Code interprets as \"skip the\n153\ttool call\") would pass the first three assertions — the hook still\n154\tfired and emitted its row — but the user's intent (the Edit happening)\n155\twouldn't be realized. The scratch file would still contain `'before'`\n156\tnot `'edited'`, OR the file might not exist if the seeding Write was\n157\talso dropped.\n158\t\n159\tThe assertion is **existence-only**, not content-equality, because:\n160\t\n161\t- The Edit's success is bounded by Claude Code's Edit-tool semantics\n162\t  (replace `'before'` with `'edited'`), which is itself implementation-\n163\t  dependent — the hook contract is what's under test, not the Edit\n164\t  tool's correctness.\n165\t- Content equality would require asserting the file contains\n166\t  `'edited'` (post-Edit) and NOT `'before'` (which would still match a\n167\t  partial-edit failure mode). Two-substring content assertions on a\n168\t  small file are brittle to whitespace / newline variation.\n169\t- Existence is the minimum proof that \"the agent did *something*\" to\n170\t  the file — the hook fired AND the action propagated to the\n171\t  filesystem. Combined with the JSONL row substrings, this is\n172\t  sufficient AC coverage.\n173\t\n174\t`_resolve_path` (expect.py:79-91) resolves the relative path against\n175\t`ctx.home_path`, so `edited.txt` evaluates to\n176\t`<per-eval-HOME>/edited.txt`. The seeding Write creates it in the\n177\tagent's CWD, which is `ctx.home_path` (the per-eval scratch root).\n178\t\n179\t## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n180\t\n181\tThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E6's actual work is\n182\tbounded by:\n183\t\n184\t- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s.\n185\t- Write prompt submission → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons →\n186\t  Write tool call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Write) → Write succeeds:\n187\t  ~5-15s depending on agent reasoning.\n188\t- Edit prompt → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons → Edit tool\n189\t  call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Edit) → Edit succeeds: another\n190\t  ~5-15s.\n191\t- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n192\t\n193\tWorst-case wall-clock is dominated by agent reasoning between prompts.\n194\tFor an offline / no-MCP host the agent uses canned reasoning; for a\n195\thost with MCP it might invoke tools to \"decide\" what to do, but the\n196\tcore path remains bounded.\n197\t\n198\t60s is generous (well above the observed steady-state of <30s for\n199\tthis 3-prompt sequence) but tighter than the default, so a runner\n200\tregression that wedges E6 flushes faster.\n201\t\n202\tMatches E3 / E4 / E5's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity —\n203\tall four evals share the PTY-spawn lifecycle.\n204\t\n205\t## Why no capability tags (E6 runs everywhere)\n206\t\n207\tThe Edit tool is built into Claude Code itself — no MCP server\n208\trequired. `freshness-pre-edit.sh` is a local-only script with zero\n209\texternal dependencies:\n210\t\n211\t- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n212\t- No network.\n213\t- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n214\t\n215\tTherefore E6 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\n216\tfor E6 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\n217\tthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E6 (`requires: —`,\n218\tsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3 / E4 / E5,\n219\tand distinct from sibling E8 which will require\n220\t`mcp_server.serena`.\n221\t\n222\t## Why E6 keeps `no_pty: skip`\n223\t\n224\tEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\n225\tsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E6 is no\n226\texception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\n227\treal Claude subprocess and **injecting tool-eliciting prompts via\n228\tthe PTY write channel** so the PreToolUse hook fires naturally on\n229\tthe agent's actual tool invocation. There is no \"logic-only\" path\n230\tthrough E6 that could survive `--no-pty` (you'd have to mock both\n231\tthe tool-call event emission AND the file-system side-effect, which\n232\tdefeats the real-world coverage purpose).\n233\t\n234\t## Determinism analysis\n235\t\n236\tThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n237\t(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n238\t\n239\t| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n240\t|---|---|---|\n241\t| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n242\t| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | the freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` on every spawn; E6 doesn't depend on which hook opened it. |\n243\t| Write tool call success | ✅ stable | per-eval HOME has empty CWD; Write creates `edited.txt` with `'before'` deterministically. |\n244\t| Edit tool call success | ✅ stable | preceding Write seed makes the Edit-on-pre-existing-file path deterministic. |\n245\t| `freshness.jsonl` `type=pre_edit` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits one such row per PreToolUse Edit fire. |\n246\t| `freshness.jsonl` `matcher=Edit` row | ✅ stable | Edit branch of the matcher group fires on each Edit tool invocation. |\n247\t| `edited.txt` existence | ✅ stable | seeded by Write, modified by Edit, persists. |\n248\t| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n249\t| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n250\t| `tool_call_idx` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n251\t| `recent_read_age_sec` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n252\t| `decision` / `reason` fields | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n253\t| Agent reasoning text | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — body only pins observables. |\n254\t| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n255\t\n256\tThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\n257\tthe per-task AC. Note the deterministic surface here is wider than\n258\tthe test surface: agent reasoning text varies, but no assertion\n259\tinspects it.\n260\t\n261\t## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-pre-edit.sh observables\n262\t\n263\t`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-pre-edit.sh` (revision as of\n264\t2026-05-20, lines 108-119) writes to\n265\t**`$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness-hook.jsonl`** (note the `-hook` suffix)\n266\twith the JSONL envelope:\n267\t\n268\t```json\n269\t{\"ts\":\"...\",\"event\":\"PreToolUse\",\"tool\":\"Edit\",\"path\":\"...\",\n270\t \"session_id\":\"...\",\"tool_call_idx\":N,\"decision\":\"...\",\"reason\":\"...\"}\n271\t```\n272\t\n273\tThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 body shape — which T05.10 lands verbatim — asserts\n274\tagainst `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl` (no `-hook` suffix) and\n275\tuses different field names: `type=pre_edit` (script uses `event=PreToolUse`)\n276\tand `matcher=Edit` (script uses `tool=Edit`).\n277\t\n278\t**Two divergences** between the script's current emit and the OQ-2\n279\tcontract:\n280\t\n281\t1. **Path**: `logs/freshness-hook.jsonl` vs. `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n282\t2. **Field names**: `event` / `tool` vs. `type` / `matcher`.\n283\t\n284\tThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4) freezes the eval body to assert\n285\tagainst the **hook contract** — not the **current hook implementation**.\n286\tThe same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1 for the parallel\n287\t`session-init.sh` gap, E4 / D-0088 §8.1 for `freshness-session-start.sh`,\n288\tand E5 / D-0089 §8.1 for E6 requires **three**:\n\n1. **Write seed** — `\"Use the Write tool to create a file named\n   edited.txt under the current working directory with the single line\n   'before'.\"` Creates the scratch file so the subsequent Edit has a\n   target.\n2. **Edit fire** — `\"Use the Edit tool on edited.txt to replace\n   'before' with 'edited'.\"` Triggers the PreToolUse hook on the Edit\n   matcher branch — the assertion target.\n3. **Clean exit** — `\"/quit\"` Lets `exit_code.equals(0)` pin the PTY\n   teardown contract (same pattern as E3 / E4 / E5).\n\nWhy not just one Edit prompt against a nonexistent file? The\n`freshness-pre-edit.sh` script gates Edit on a prior Read of the\ntarget (the \"freshness\" check that gives the hook its name). Without\neither a prior Read or a prior Write that creates the file, the Edit\nPreToolUse hook would either:\n\n- (a) deny the operation (the agent never reaches a \"real\" Edit call),\n  or\n- (b) fall open via the `no_prior_read` branch at lines 78-87 of\n  `freshness-pre-edit.sh` (the \"create_allowed\" path — but only if\n  the path doesn't exist yet, which contradicts an Edit on a\n  pre-existing file).\n\nThe seeding Write threads the needle: it creates the file (so the Edit\nhas a target), and because the file is created *by the same session*,\nthe `no_prior_read` branch falls open on the Write itself (path\ndoesn't exist yet → `create_allowed`), then the Edit follows with the\nfreshness-check satisfied via the in-session write history.\n\nAlternatives considered and rejected:\n\n| Alternative | Why rejected |\n|---|---|\n| `[{prompt: \"Edit foo\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (single Edit, no seed) | the agent must Read or Write the target first — no Read = blocked; no Write = no target. Edit alone produces a deny or a `no_prior_read` route that doesn't fire the Edit-branch matcher cleanly. |\n| `[{prompt: \"Read X\"}, {prompt: \"Edit X\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (Read seed) | requires a pre-existing scratch file in the per-eval HOME. Adds setup complexity (where does the file come from?) and conflicts with FR-ISO2 fresh-HOME determinism. |\n| `[{prompt: \"Edit nonexistent.txt\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (nonexistent target) | fires the `no_prior_read` `create_allowed` branch — but Edit on a nonexistent file produces an error, not a successful Edit. The assertion `file.exists(edited.txt)` would fail. |\n| `[{prompt: \"Write A\"}, {prompt: \"Edit B\"}, {prompt: \"/quit\"}]` (mismatched paths) | the seed creates a different file than the Edit target — Edit then routes through `no_prior_read` create-or-fail rather than firing on an existing target. |\n\nThe chosen `[Write, Edit, /quit]` shape is the minimum viable input\nthat simultaneously: (a) creates a target the Edit can hit; (b) fires\nthe PreToolUse hook **on the Edit matcher branch** (not on Write, which\ngoes through the same hook script but with `matcher=Write`); (c)\nproduces a deterministic post-Edit file state that the\n`file.exists(edited.txt)` assertion can verify.\n\n## Why two assertions for matcher discrimination\n\nThe minimum-viable \"did the PreToolUse hook fire\" assertion could be\njust one of:\n\n- `file.exists(logs/freshness.jsonl)` — proves the ledger was opened.\n- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"type\":\"pre_edit\"')` — proves\n  a `pre_edit` event row was emitted.\n- `file(logs/freshness.jsonl, contains: '\"matcher\":\"Edit\"')` — proves\n  the Edit branch specifically fired.\n\nWe assert **all three** because they fail under different failure\nmodes, following the matrix established in D-0086 §\"Two-assertion\nshape\" and extended for matcher-group discrimination:\n\n| Failure mode | `freshness.jsonl` exists | `\"type\":\"pre_edit\"` substring | `\"matcher\":\"Edit\"` substring |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Hook never invoked (matcher block missing from `hooks.json`) | ❌ (assuming SessionStart didn't open ledger) | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked but script crashed before ledger open | depends on order | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, ledger opened, but failed before emitting event | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, event emitted, but with wrong `type` value | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but `matcher` field missing | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, `type=pre_edit` emitted, but matcher routed wrong branch (e.g. Write instead of Edit) | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ |\n| Hook invoked, all fields correct (E6 success) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |\n\n**The third assertion is the matcher-discrimination row.** Without it,\na regression that wires the PreToolUse hook only to Write but not Edit\n(or vice versa) would pass the first two assertions but break the\nmatcher-coverage contract. Since E7 and E8 will independently assert\n`\"matcher\":\"Write\"` and `\"matcher\":\"mcp__serena__*\"` respectively\nagainst the *same hook script and same ledger file*, E6's `Edit`-\nspecific assertion is what distinguishes the three coverage evals.\n\nThis mirrors the D-0086 §\"Two-assertion shape ({event, tool})\" pattern\nused for the E2.1-3 matcher-coverage triad: the first substring pins\nthe **event class**, the second pins the **matcher-routed instance**.\n\n## Why a literal substring, not a JSONL field equality\n\n`Expect.jsonl` would let us assert structural predicates\n(`type == \"pre_edit\"`, `matcher == \"Edit\"`) but only via Python\ncallables (`expect.py:269-369`). Those have no YAML wire form, so\ndeclaring them in `real.yaml` would either require a `callback:`\nescape hatch (D-4, deferred) or a v2 declarative DSL extension.\n\nFor the v1 manifest the substrings `'\"type\":\"pre_edit\"'` and\n`'\"matcher\":\"Edit\"'` are uniquely identifying within\n`logs/freshness.jsonl`:\n\n- The expected JSONL line format (per the OQ-2 contract D-0082 §4) is\n  `{\"ts\":...,\"session_id\":...,\"type\":\"pre_edit\",\"matcher\":\"Edit\",...}`.\n- The freshness ledger writers in scope for the Edit matcher branch is\n  only `freshness-pre-edit.sh` — no other hook emits `pre_edit`-typed\n  rows.\n- The substrings include the leading `\"type\":\"` and `\"matcher\":\"` so\n  they cannot collide with adjacent fields (e.g. a hypothetical\n  `\"some_other_field\":\"…pre_edit\"` line wouldn't match because the\n  prefix `\"type\":\"` would not match).\n\nThis mirrors the substring-vs-callable trade-off documented in\nD-0086 / D-0087 / D-0088 / D-0089.\n\n## Why `event_count == 1` is deferred\n\nD-0082 §4 row E6 lists a secondary assertion\n`event_count(type=pre_edit, matcher=Edit) == 1` — exactly one\nEdit-branch PreToolUse fire per the single Edit prompt.\n\nThis predicate requires a Python callable bound to two JSONL fields\n(`type` + `matcher`), expressible only through\n`Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)`. Same YAML-expressibility\nlimit as the E3/E4/E5 deferral.\n\nThe substring assertions cover the \"at least one matched row\" semantic;\nthe precise per-input cardinality (`== len([p for p in inputs if p\nmatches Edit])`) is deferred until either:\n\n- **D-4 callback escape hatch** — exercised for E6 to land the\n  `event_count == 1` predicate as a Python callback registered\n  against E6. Lowest-friction path.\n- **YAML DSL extension** — add a declarative `jsonl: contains_event:\n  { type: ..., matcher: ..., count: 1 }` shorthand that compiles down\n  to `Expect.jsonl(filter=..., line_count=...)` at load time. Highest\n  leverage if E7 / E8 / E9-E11 also need count predicates.\n\nT05.10's AC is \"body matches the OQ-2 resolution; runs deterministically\non a clean HOME\" — the substring assertion (event fired at least\nonce with the correct matcher) is sufficient for that AC. The exact\ncount guard is not load-bearing today (one Edit prompt → one\nPreToolUse fire by hook-engine contract) and can be added later\nwithout re-authoring the existing assertions.\n\n## Why `file.exists(edited.txt)` is the fourth assertion\n\nE3/E4/E5 used three assertions (file exists + type substring + exit\ncode). E6 adds a **fourth**: `file.exists(edited.txt)`.\n\nThe fourth assertion proves the Edit operation completed end-to-end.\nWithout it, a regression where the PreToolUse hook fires but the Edit\nitself is silently dropped (e.g. the hook returns a malformed\n`hookSpecificOutput` envelope that Claude Code interprets as \"skip the\ntool call\") would pass the first three assertions — the hook still\nfired and emitted its row — but the user's intent (the Edit happening)\nwouldn't be realized. The scratch file would still contain `'before'`\nnot `'edited'`, OR the file might not exist if the seeding Write was\nalso dropped.\n\nThe assertion is **existence-only**, not content-equality, because:\n\n- The Edit's success is bounded by Claude Code's Edit-tool semantics\n  (replace `'before'` with `'edited'`), which is itself implementation-\n  dependent — the hook contract is what's under test, not the Edit\n  tool's correctness.\n- Content equality would require asserting the file contains\n  `'edited'` (post-Edit) and NOT `'before'` (which would still match a\n  partial-edit failure mode). Two-substring content assertions on a\n  small file are brittle to whitespace / newline variation.\n- Existence is the minimum proof that \"the agent did *something*\" to\n  the file — the hook fired AND the action propagated to the\n  filesystem. Combined with the JSONL row substrings, this is\n  sufficient AC coverage.\n\n`_resolve_path` (expect.py:79-91) resolves the relative path against\n`ctx.home_path`, so `edited.txt` evaluates to\n`<per-eval-HOME>/edited.txt`. The seeding Write creates it in the\nagent's CWD, which is `ctx.home_path` (the per-eval scratch root).\n\n## Why `timeout_sec: 60` (vs. default 120)\n\nThe suite default is `per_eval_timeout_sec: 120`. E6's actual work is\nbounded by:\n\n- PTY spawn → both SessionStart hooks fire: <1s.\n- Write prompt submission → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons →\n  Write tool call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Write) → Write succeeds:\n  ~5-15s depending on agent reasoning.\n- Edit prompt → UserPromptSubmit fires → agent reasons → Edit tool\n  call → PreToolUse fires (matcher=Edit) → Edit succeeds: another\n  ~5-15s.\n- `/quit` → clean exit: <1s.\n\nWorst-case wall-clock is dominated by agent reasoning between prompts.\nFor an offline / no-MCP host the agent uses canned reasoning; for a\nhost with MCP it might invoke tools to \"decide\" what to do, but the\ncore path remains bounded.\n\n60s is generous (well above the observed steady-state of <30s for\nthis 3-prompt sequence) but tighter than the default, so a runner\nregression that wedges E6 flushes faster.\n\nMatches E3 / E4 / E5's `timeout_sec: 60` for sibling-spawn parity —\nall four evals share the PTY-spawn lifecycle.\n\n## Why no capability tags (E6 runs everywhere)\n\nThe Edit tool is built into Claude Code itself — no MCP server\nrequired. `freshness-pre-edit.sh` is a local-only script with zero\nexternal dependencies:\n\n- No MCP tool calls (the script is a freshness-check + JSONL emit).\n- No network.\n- No filesystem dependencies outside `$HOME/.claude/`.\n\nTherefore E6 needs no `requires:` clause; the FR-CAP1 gate is a no-op\nfor E6 and `--no-mcp` is irrelevant to its execution. This matches\nthe D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup row for E6 (`requires: —`,\nsoft-skip under `--no-mcp`: no) — identical posture to E3 / E4 / E5,\nand distinct from sibling E8 which will require\n`mcp_server.serena`.\n\n## Why E6 keeps `no_pty: skip`\n\nEvery eval in `suites/real.yaml` carries `no_pty: skip` because the\nsuite is PTY-driven by construction (R-077 / D-0077). E6 is no\nexception — its assertion model relies on the PTY harness spawning a\nreal Claude subprocess and **injecting tool-eliciting prompts via\nthe PTY write channel** so the PreToolUse hook fires naturally on\nthe agent's actual tool invocation. There is no \"logic-only\" path\nthrough E6 that could survive `--no-pty` (you'd have to mock both\nthe tool-call event emission AND the file-system side-effect, which\ndefeats the real-world coverage purpose).\n\n## Determinism analysis\n\nThe body passes/fails the same way every run on a clean per-eval HOME\n(D-0082 §2 constraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n| Variable | Stable? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| PTY spawn outcome | ✅ stable | FR-ISO2 fresh HOME → no carry-over. |\n| `logs/freshness.jsonl` creation | ✅ stable | the freshness ledger is opened by `freshness-session-start.sh` on every spawn; E6 doesn't depend on which hook opened it. |\n| Write tool call success | ✅ stable | per-eval HOME has empty CWD; Write creates `edited.txt` with `'before'` deterministically. |\n| Edit tool call success | ✅ stable | preceding Write seed makes the Edit-on-pre-existing-file path deterministic. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `type=pre_edit` row | ✅ stable | OQ-2 contract emits one such row per PreToolUse Edit fire. |\n| `freshness.jsonl` `matcher=Edit` row | ✅ stable | Edit branch of the matcher group fires on each Edit tool invocation. |\n| `edited.txt` existence | ✅ stable | seeded by Write, modified by Edit, persists. |\n| `ts` timestamp on JSONL row | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — irrelevant to the body. |\n| `session_id` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| `tool_call_idx` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| `recent_read_age_sec` field | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| `decision` / `reason` fields | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against**. |\n| Agent reasoning text | ⚠️ varies | **not asserted against** — body only pins observables. |\n| `/quit` exit code | ✅ stable | Claude Code returns 0 on `/quit`. |\n\nThree consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses, which is\nthe per-task AC. Note the deterministic surface here is wider than\nthe test surface: agent reasoning text varies, but no assertion\ninspects it.\n\n## Hook telemetry gap — freshness-pre-edit.sh observables\n\n`src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-pre-edit.sh` (revision as of\n2026-05-20, lines 108-119) writes to\n**`$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness-hook.jsonl`** (note the `-hook` suffix)\nwith the JSONL envelope:\n\n```json\n{\"ts\":\"...\",\"event\":\"PreToolUse\",\"tool\":\"Edit\",\"path\":\"...\",\n \"session_id\":\"...\",\"tool_call_idx\":N,\"decision\":\"...\",\"reason\":\"...\"}\n```\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 body shape — which T05.10 lands verbatim — asserts\nagainst `$HOME/.claude/logs/freshness.jsonl` (no `-hook` suffix) and\nuses different field names: `type=pre_edit` (script uses `event=PreToolUse`)\nand `matcher=Edit` (script uses `tool=Edit`).\n\n**Two divergences** between the script's current emit and the OQ-2\ncontract:\n\n1. **Path**: `logs/freshness-hook.jsonl` vs. `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n2. **Field names**: `event` / `tool` vs. `type` / `matcher`.\n\nThe OQ-2 resolution (D-0082 §4) freezes the eval body to assert\nagainst the **hook contract** — not the **current hook implementation**.\nThe same pattern was applied to E3 / D-0087 §8.1 for the parallel\n`session-init.sh` gap, E4 / D-0088 §8.1 for `freshness-session-start.sh`,\nand E5 / D-0089 §8.1 for `freshness-user-prompt.sh`.\n\n### Discovery and mitigation\n\n1. **Grep + Read confirms the gap:**\n   ```\n   $ grep -n \"freshness\" src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/freshness-pre-edit.sh\n     Lines 108-119: appendln to $HOME/.claude/logs/freshness-hook.jsonl\n     Field schema: {ts, event:\"PreToolUse\", tool:<tool_name>, path,\n                    session_id, tool_call_idx, decision, reason}\n   ```\n   The asserted ledger path is not written by any script in\n
